{"parentUuid":null,"isSidechain":true,"promptId":"8a1bd755-fe55-49e4-aa04-1c9cb10eb027","agentId":"a42954e2ff29371d9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":"You are RED-PEN #2, an adversarial reviewer for the SIGIL spine at /home/kali/sigil (git repo). Find real defects and BLOCK the merge; do not fix, do not praise. Be concrete.\n\nUnder review: Slice 4 — gzip-on-seal (compaction) — commit `4f3c58b`. Read:\n  cd /home/kali/sigil && git show 4f3c58b --stat && git show 4f3c58b -- sigil/spine/store.py\nFull file sigil/spine/store.py (focus: the gz-aware read helpers _open_segment, _scan_segment, _read_segment_tail, _last_line_of; get() over a gz segment; the segment-aware index with mixed plaintext+gz). Invariants brief: /tmp/claude-1000/-home-kali-Pictures-PENTEST-main/7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27/scratchpad/spine_design_brief.md.\n\nYOUR LENS — GZIP CORRECTNESS + INDEX + TAMPER-MODEL over a mixed plaintext/gz segment set:\n1. **Decompressed-offset index correctness.** For a gz segment, _scan_segment reads the whole DECOMPRESSED stream and stores decompressed byte offsets; get() does `_open_segment(gz).seek(off).readline()`. Verify gzip.GzipFile.seek(off) + readline() returns EXACTLY the record at decompressed offset `off` (build a multi-record gz segment, index it, get() every seq, compare to a full scan). Any off-by-one, any record whose decompressed offset the scan computes wrong?\n2. **The reconcile with a gz segment.** _ensure_index's sealed branch is `elif scanned < size` where size = p.stat().st_size = the COMPRESSED size, but _seg_scanned[gz] is set to the DECOMPRESSED length. Trace: first pass scanned=0 < compressed_size → scan (good); after, _seg_scanned=decompressed_size. Next pass: is `decompressed_size < compressed_size`? For a well-compressed segment decompressed>compressed so it's skipped (good) — but for a segment that gzip made LARGER (tiny/incompressible), decompressed<compressed → it RE-SCANS every reconcile (perf) — or worse, does re-scanning corrupt _offsets/_max_seq? Also: does a gz segment ever hit the ACTIVE branch (size>scanned extend) wrongly?\n3. **verify()/tamper-model over gz.** verify() streams all segments via iter_records → _open_segment decompresses gz → feeds verify_chain. Confirm a gzip'd segment yields byte-identical records (so cert_digest binding + entry_hash chain still verify) and that entry_count/the signed head are unchanged by compaction. Sign a head, compact, and re-verify_checkpoint. Then: can a TAMPERED gz segment (edit a record, re-gzip) be caught? (verify() re-hashes from decompressed bytes, so yes — confirm.) Is the manifest `sha256`/`bytes` for a gz segment used in any tamper decision (it must NOT be)?\n4. **_read_segment_tail / _last_line_of on gz.** tail() over a gz segment decompresses fully + takes last k. _last_line_of(gz) decompresses + last valid line. Correct vs plaintext? Any case (empty gz, gz with a trailing newline, gz whose last line is malformed) where they differ from the plaintext path or return a wrong tip (which would regress next_seq / the seam boundary)?\n5. **Mixed-codec spine consumers.** Build a migrated+rotated+compacted spine holding security records (kill-switch engage in an early gz segment, nonce receipts, device auth). Confirm the full-scanners (killswitch _scan_engaged, envelope.device_nonce_highwater, mesh.authorized_devices, actor_scope.creation_allowed) reconstruct the SAME value over the gz+plaintext set as over a single plaintext file. Any consumer that breaks on a gz segment?\n6. **_open_segment codec detection by suffix.** It keys on `p.suffix == \".gz\"`. Is the suffix always correct (manifest `file` for gz is `...seg-NNNNN.jsonl.gz`; segment_filename(id,\"gzip\"))? Could a plaintext segment ever be opened as gz or vice-versa (wrong codec → decompress error / garbage)? Does the manifest `codec` field and the file suffix ever disagree?\n7. **gzip determinism / at-rest.** gzip embeds an mtime by default → non-deterministic .gz bytes. Does anything hash or compare the .gz bytes (it must not — only the DECOMPRESSED content is chain-relevant)? Confirm the manifest sha256 (if set) isn't a chain input.\n\nBuild real mixed-codec spines to check (temp SIGIL_HOME, injected small thresholds, then compact()). Run the suite if useful. Report a ranked list of real findings (file:line, concrete input, consequence), distinguishing real defect from note. Ground claims in code you read."},"uuid":"45397129-1781-4817-b102-262863baaba0","timestamp":"2026-07-19T18:54:10.493Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

Use it when a task names CRUCIBLE, OBSIDIAN, `python3 -m framework.v2 <subcommand>`, findings/oracles/evidence certificates, the blackboard event spine, critics, reflection, or the reward/calibration loop.\n- deep-research: Deep research harness — fan-out web searches, fetch sources, adversarially verify claims, synthesize a cited report. - When the user wants a deep, multi-source, fact-checked research report on any topic. On the email-auth stream it caught 3 CRITICAL/LOW defects PAST a RED-PEN PASS — near-zero-FP cannot be self-certified by one reviewer.\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".\n- artifact-design: Design guidance and fundamentals for Artifacts.\n- artifact-capabilities: Runtime capabilities a published Artifact page can be granted — behavior static HTML cannot provide on its own, such as the page reading live or connected data, keeping state shared across viewers, or updating and republishing itself. Serves this user's live capability roster and the typed call definitions. Load it whenever the user asks for an artifact needing any such runtime behavior.\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- verify: Verify that a code change actually does what it's supposed to by exercising it end-to-end and observing behavior — drive the affected flow, not just tests or typecheck. Run before committing nontrivial changes; bootstraps this repo's project verify skill if none exists yet. Don't invoke it on a diff that only touches tests, docs, or other code with no runtime surface to drive (a change to product source always has one) — there's nothing to observe.\n- code-review: Review the current diff for correctness bugs and reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings; ultra: deep multi-agent review in the cloud). Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review.\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. First looks for a project skill that already covers launching the app; otherwise falls back to built-in patterns per project type (CLI, server, TUI, Electron, browser-driven, library).\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a GitHub pull request; for your working diff use /code-review\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch","skillCount":19,"isInitial":true,"names":["crucible","deep-research","adversarial-sweep","dataviz","artifact-design","artifact-capabilities","update-config","keybindings-help","verify","code-review","simplify","fewer-permission-prompts","loop","schedule","claude-api","run","init","review","security-review"]},"type":"attachment","uuid":"e2bac433-35db-4e7c-a335-592a1ae5756d","timestamp":"2026-07-19T18:54:10.510Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"4dc36faa-5214-4260-8afe-9e1454bcc1df","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"8a1bd755-fe55-49e4-aa04-1c9cb10eb027","agentId":"a42954e2ff29371d9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01C7XzBx9Fv3J3dwAkePnCNc","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# SIGIL spine bounded-growth — DESIGN BRIEF (distilled from the understand phase)\n2\t\n3\tThe SIGIL spine is an append-only, hash-chained, Ed25519-signed JSONL log at `~/.sigil/spine/spine.jsonl`\n4\t(93 MB / 43,412 records, growing). GOAL: bound its growth / per-file size / read latency **without\n5\tweakening tamper-evidence or the append-only guarantee**. 34 consumers across 464 call sites read it;\n6\t23 do FULL scans; 9 need old-seq resolution. Full raw analysis (invariants, per-consumer contract,\n7\ttamper-conflict, durability, critique) is in `/tmp/claude-1000/-home-kali-Pictures-PENTEST-main/7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27/tasks/w705miq65.output` — read it for detail. Code is at `/home/kali/sigil/sigil/spine/*` and `sigil/reuse/{chain,canonical,models}.py`.\n8\t\n9\t## The 19 invariants a design MUST preserve (condensed)\n10\t\n11\t1. **chain-anchored-from-genesis** — `verify_chain` seeds `prev=_GENESIS_PREV` and rejects entries[0] if\n12\t   its prev_hash != genesis. (It does NOT require entries[0].seq==0 — only prev==genesis + contiguity —\n13\t   so a rebased chain at seq K is valid IFF K's prev_hash is rewritten to a genesis-equivalent value.)\n14\t2. **strict-seq-contiguity** — seq[i]==seq[i-1]+1, no gaps/dupes. Prune may only remove a CONTIGUOUS\n15\t   PREFIX, never punch interior holes.\n16\t3. **seq-is-immutable-primary-key** — never renumber; entry_hash binds seq; parent_id/supersedes_id/\n17\t   cursors/graph anchors reference seq. Keep original seqs across any compaction.\n18\t4. **signed-head-entry_count-is-the-anti-truncation-gate** — `classify_head` flags `len(entries) <\n19\t   head.entry_count` as TAMPERING; entry_count is ABSOLUTE. THE central conflict: prune ⇒ shorter ⇒ reads\n20\t   as rollback. Any prune MUST re-sign a snapshot-aware head atomically.\n21\t5. **monotonic-last_seq anti-rollback** — tip seq never decreases (verify_head prev_highwater [dormant on\n22\t   checkpoint path]; SpineTailer._high_water in-memory). Front-prune keeps tip seq ⇒ safe; renumber ⇒\n23\t   false trip + real-truncation-indistinguishable.\n24\t6. **append-only / offsets-never-move / no-in-place-rewrite** — never rewrite in place; build-then-\n25\t   os.replace. In-place same-or-larger rewrite is UNDETECTED by the size-only index heuristic ⇒ silent\n26\t   wrong reads.\n27\t7. **torn-tail-truncate-before-append (BLOCK-1)** — must run on the ACTIVE append segment only; a sealed\n28\t   segment that ends torn will never be truncated by a future append ⇒ permanent false verify() gap\n29\t   (critique A2: rotation must run `_last_valid_boundary` truncate at SEAL time).\n30\t8. **fsync durability** — acked append fsync'd; head swapped via temp+fsync+os.replace+dir-fsync\n31\t   (`checkpoint._atomic_write_text`). Every rotation output must match this.\n32\t9. **index extend/rebuild correctness under external mutation** — detection is SIZE-ONLY today; must\n33\t   become IDENTITY/EPOCH-based (st_dev/st_ino + manifest generation), and segment-aware (key by\n34\t   (segment,offset)). Rotation must not read as an in-place shrink (which is a truncation attack).\n35\t10. **replay-stable content digest (ts excluded)** — any snapshot/manifest record must digest via the\n36\t    same `append()` content shape + canonical_json (ts excluded) or its binding check fails.\n37\t11. **two-layer unkeyed integrity vs the keyed signed head** — a compactor is a recompute-capable writer;\n38\t    verify() alone can't certify a compaction. The owner-signed head is the authority; compaction must be\n39\t    owner-key-gated.\n40\t12. **read-path presents ONE contiguous genesis chain** — entries()/iter_records(-1)/verify()/count()\n41\t    must transparently concatenate snapshot-front + sealed segments + active segment in seq order, no\n42\t    gap/dup at a seam, so all 34 consumers keep working unchanged.\n43\t13. **durable tip determines next_seq (no seq reuse)** — `_read_last_entry`/next_seq must resolve the tip\n44\t    from the ACTIVE segment (true global max seq).\n45\t14. **cross-writer serialization must cover the compactor** — spine_lock (RLock) + flock. CRITICAL: flock\n46\t    binds to the INODE, not the path; os.replace swaps the inode, so flock-on-target CANNOT exclude a\n47\t    cross-process appender. Need a PATH-STABLE lockfile (`spine.lock`) both append & compaction flock\n48\t    before resolving the active segment.\n49\t15. **monotonic-state-by-full-scan must survive** — per-device nonce high-water (envelope.py), newest\n50\t    warden_checkpoint per pubkey (cli.py), account.create cap count (actor_scope/actor), device\n51\t    authorize/revoke ledger (mesh/registry), kill-switch latch (killswitch), promotion grants\n52\t    (promotion), unresolved queued approvals (approvals), archivist supersession + promotion-key ledger\n53\t    (revise), open threads/commitments/contradictions + citations (queries.py). Rotation (retain-all)\n54\t    keeps these IF scans span segments; PRUNE would silently corrupt them unless each is folded into the\n55\t    signed snapshot.\n56\t16. **referential integrity of parent_id/supersedes_id across a prune** — never dangle; never mutate a\n57\t    retained record's parent/supersedes (breaks its binding).\n58\t17. **canonical/domain primitives frozen** — snapshot/head schema changes need a schema_version bump +\n59\t    migration, computed by the same reuse functions.\n60\t18. **single signed head is the commit point** — extend to carry a snapshot base coherently; keep ONE\n61\t    atomically-published head.\n62\t19. **genesis-vs-empty count semantics** — a compacted store is never \"empty\"; next_seq/last_seq must\n63\t    derive from the snapshot base, not physical len==0.\n64\t\n65\t## Critique additions (must be in any design)\n66\t- **A1 closed head schema** — `SignedChainHead`/`ChainEntry` are `extra=\"forbid\"`. A v2 head with snapshot\n67\t  fields is a BREAKING wire migration; today the two head-consumers fail DIFFERENTLY (SpineTailer swallows\n68\t  ⇒ silent un-anchored; verify_checkpoint has no try/except ⇒ crashes `sigil verify` + mcp ingest_status).\n69\t- **A3 next_seq is cached** — a long-lived store instance compacted by another process keeps stale\n70\t  next_seq; must invalidate on epoch change.\n71\t- **A4 size-as-change-token in THREE places** — store._ensure_index, killswitch.py:57, gesture/session.py:121.\n72\t  A same-size rotation serves a STALE kill-switch verdict in the un-halting direction. Fix must be UNIFIED\n73\t  (shared epoch token).\n74\t- **Missing consumers** — consolidate/queries.py (full-scan, citation-heavy MCP surface — first-class),\n75\t  consolidate/gate.py (get(seq) grounding), ingest/transcript.py (sets parent_id ⇒ referential integrity).\n76\t- **C1 (decisive)** — NO durable anti-rollback floor lives OUTSIDE the compactable data. Hard-prune turns\n77\t  the `n<signed_n` truncation alarm into a no-op for the pruned range, so a cold/fresh verifier can't\n78\t  distinguish a legit compaction-baseline from a replayed old (short-spine, low-count-head) pair. Closing\n79\t  this needs a durable external floor (per-verifier local last-accepted last_seq, and/or authenticated\n80\t  head delivery a mirror can't roll back). WARM same-host verifiers are fine; COLD/fresh are not.\n81\t- **C2** — once classify_head slices only the LIVE window, the pruned prefix is NO LONGER re-hashed, so the\n82\t  snapshot's committed digest MUST be a Merkle/hash commitment binding EVERY pruned record (else a pre-\n83\t  archival edit is unprovable). This is an invariant, not a nice-to-have.\n84\t- **D1** — must NOT hold the append lock across the O(file) heavy copy (would block a kill-switch PANIC\n85\t  append). Heavy copy of immutable records is lock-free; only a bounded seal→publish critical section\n86\t  takes the lock.\n87\t- **D2** — live-reader vs unlink race across the manifest indirection: readers must snapshot ALL segment\n88\t  fds right after reading the manifest; unlink deferred behind a reader grace/refcount.\n89\t\n90\t## Cutover protocol (durability analysis — the required shape)\n91\tONE authoritative **manifest** naming the active segment set = the sole linearization point, swapped via\n92\t`_atomic_write_text` (temp→fsync→os.replace→dir-fsync). Ordering: write+fsync new segment(s) → write+fsync\n93\tnew manifest → os.replace manifest (the atomic instant) → dir-fsync → ONLY THEN unlink superseded segments.\n94\tentries()/count() derive the set from the MANIFEST, never a directory glob (glob double-counts during the\n95\tunlink window). Manifest swap + head swap must be crash-consistent together (a crash between them yields a\n96\tfalse TAMPERING window). Seal-time torn-tail truncate on the sealed segment. Carry seq/entry_hash across\n97\tthe seam so verify_chain's contiguity+linkage never break.\n98\t\n99\t## The 5 questions the DESIGN must answer\n100\t1. Where does the durable, non-replayable anti-rollback floor live? (C1) — only relevant if PRUNING.\n101\t2. Exact SignedChainHead schema migration + version-negotiation (old mobile clients / sigil verify vs a v2 head)?\n102\t3. The atomic multi-file commit protocol + which bounded critical section holds the lock (D1/D2/D3)?\n103\t4. Which record classes are un-prunable state-bearers and how is each folded into the signed snapshot? (only if pruning)\n104\t5. Honest contract for resolving a pruned seq (positive \"compacted below N, see snapshot X\" vs a bare 404)?\n105\t\n106\t## Key strategic observation (for the panel to validate or challenge)\n107\tRetain-all SEGMENT ROTATION needs NONE of {C1 durable floor, snapshot state-folding, head-schema prune\n108\tsemantics} — it keeps entry_count absolute, the chain genesis-rooted, all 34 consumers transparent (via a\n109\tsegment-spanning read API), and reclaims practical disk via gzip-on-seal (JSONL ~5-10x). It touches only\n110\tstorage mechanics (manifest, lockfile, epoch-index, cutover) — the HARD-but-BOUNDED engineering. HARD-PRUNE\n111\t(space reclaim by deleting records) is where C1 + state-folding + a breaking head migration live — a\n112\ttamper-evidence tradeoff that is an OPERATOR decision, not an autonomous one.\n113\t"}]},"uuid":"7270efae-c6bc-4f6b-a196-ab7dab3e8536","timestamp":"2026-07-19T18:54:15.769Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"4dc36faa-5214-4260-8afe-9e1454bcc1df","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

The active\n      segment is always plaintext.\n    \n    Measured ~4x reclaim on a repetitive test payload (production hash-JSONL compresses ~5-8x).\n    \n    Tests (+5): compact gzips sealed + reads transparently (verify/get/tail/count/iter across gz+plaintext) +\n    idempotent; D2 reader-grace under 4 concurrent readers vs interleaved compact (no ENOENT, always a\n    contiguous chain); reap respects the grace; crash-before-flip recovers; orphan-plaintext swept after a\n    post-flip crash. 398 passed; ruff + mypy clean.\n    \n    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\n\ndiff --git a/sigil/spine/store.py b/sigil/spine/store.py\nindex ffabedc..71ddace 100644\n--- a/sigil/spine/store.py\n+++ b/sigil/spine/store.py\n@@ -8,16 +8,24 @@ are O(1): read the last line's entry, `append_entry`, write.\n \"\"\"\n from __future__ import annotations\n \n+import gzip\n import json\n import logging\n import os\n import shutil\n import threading\n+import time\n from contextlib import contextmanager\n from pathlib import Path\n from typing import Any, Iterator\n \n-from ..config import SCOPE, SPINE_PATH, SPINE_SEG_MAX_BYTES, SPINE_SEG_MAX_RECORDS\n+from ..config import (\n+    SCOPE,\n+    SPINE_PATH,\n+    SPINE_SEG_MAX_BYTES,\n+    SPINE_SEG_MAX_RECORDS,\n+    SPINE_TRASH_GRACE_SEC,\n+)\n from ..reuse import ChainEntry, append_entry, build_chain, digest_payload, verify_chain\n from .atomicio import fsync_dir\n from .manifest import (\n@@ -271,12 +279,9 @@ class SpineStore:\n             return None                              # genesis: a fresh spine correctly starts at seq 0\n         sealed = m.sealed_in_order()\n         if sealed:\n-            sp = self._layout.seg_path(sealed[-1])\n-            line = _last_nonempty_line(sp) if sp.exists() else None\n-            if line:\n-                d = json.loads(line)\n-                return ChainEntry(seq=d[\"seq\"], prev_hash=d[\"prev_hash\"], cert_digest=d[\"cert_digest\"],\n-                                  entry_hash=d[\"entry_hash\"])\n+            entry = self._entry_from_line(self._last_line_of(self._layout.seg_path(sealed[-1])))\n+            if entry is not None:\n+                return entry\n         raise SpineError(\n             f\"active segment first_seq={act.first_seq} is non-genesis but its seam boundary (the prior \"\n             f\"sealed segment) is unreadable — refusing to fork the chain at seq 0\")\n@@ -553,6 +558,100 @@ class SpineStore:\n                     return False\n                 return self._seal_active_locked(m, act, self._layout.seg_path(act))\n \n+    # --- compaction (gzip-on-seal; retain-all — no record removed) -----------------\n+    def compact(self) -> int:\n+        \"\"\"Compress every SEALED plaintext segment to gzip. Retain-all: no record is removed, and a `.gz` is\n+        byte-decompress-identical, so verify()/the signed head are unaffected. Reclaims ~5-8x on JSONL. The\n+        heavy gzip runs LOCK-FREE (a sealed segment is immutable — no append is blocked); only the tiny\n+        per-segment manifest codec-flip takes the flock. The superseded plaintext is MOVED to trash/ (never\n+        unlinked under a reader), and a reaper deletes trash older than the read-grace. Idempotent (a no-op\n+        on already-gzip / legacy). Returns the number compressed.\"\"\"\n+        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n+        if m is None:\n+            return 0\n+        n = 0\n+        for seg in m.sealed_in_order():\n+            if seg.codec == \"none\" and self._compress_one_sealed(seg.id):\n+                n += 1\n+        self._trash_orphan_plaintext()               # backstop: a crash between flip and move leaves plaintext\n+        self._reap_trash()\n+        return n\n+\n+    def _compress_one_sealed(self, seg_id: int) -> bool:\n+        \"\"\"P1 (lock-free gzip of the immutable sealed segment) + P2 (brief-locked manifest codec flip).\n+        Crash-safe: the .gz is built to a temp then atomically renamed; the codec flip is the atomic commit;\n+        the plaintext is left for _trash_orphan_plaintext. A crash before the flip leaves an orphan .gz that\n+        the next run overwrites; the plaintext (still referenced) is never at risk.\"\"\"\n+        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n+        seg = next((s for s in (m.segments if m else []) if s.id == seg_id and s.sealed and s.codec == \"none\"), None)\n+        if seg is None:\n+            return False\n+        plain = self._layout.seg_path(seg)\n+        if not plain.exists():\n+            return False\n+        gz_rel = self._layout.segment_rel(seg_id, \"gzip\")\n+        gz_abs = self._layout.spine_dir / gz_rel\n+        tmp = gz_abs.with_name(gz_abs.name + \".tmp\")\n+        with plain.open(\"rb\") as fin, gzip.open(tmp, \"wb\", compresslevel=6) as fout:\n+            shutil.copyfileobj(fin, fout)\n+        with open(tmp, \"rb\") as f:\n+            os.fsync(f.fileno())\n+        os.replace(tmp, gz_abs)\n+        fsync_dir(self._layout.segments_dir)\n+        with spine_lock(self.path):\n+            with self._crossproc_lock():\n+                m2 = read_manifest(self._layout)\n+                if m2 is None:\n+                    return False\n+                flipped, segs2 = False, []\n+                for s in m2.segments:\n+                    if s.id == seg_id and s.sealed and s.codec == \"none\":\n+                        segs2.append(s.model_copy(update={\"codec\": \"gzip\", \"file\": gz_rel,\n+                                                          \"bytes\": gz_abs.stat().st_size}))\n+                        flipped = True\n+                    else:\n+                        segs2.append(s)\n+                if not flipped:\n+                    return False                     # another process already flipped it\n+                write_manifest(self._layout, Manifest(generation=m2.generation + 1, scope=m2.scope, segments=segs2))\n+                self._manifest = read_manifest(self._layout)\n+                with self._index_lock:\n+                    self._index_epoch = None         #
