CLAMPED to >= 0: a negative value (e.g. an\n171\t        # operator using -1 to mean \"off\") must not read as `size >= -1` == always-true, which would seal on\n172\t        # EVERY append — a self-DoS (one segment + full manifest rewrite per record).\n173\t        self._seg_max_bytes = max(0, SPINE_SEG_MAX_BYTES if seg_max_bytes is None else seg_max_bytes)\n174\t        self._seg_max_records = max(0, SPINE_SEG_MAX_RECORDS if seg_max_records is None else seg_max_records)\n175\t        # `self.path` is the STABLE identity + lock token (…/spine.jsonl). After a migration it is renamed\n176\t        # away and no longer a data file, but it remains the key for the in-process RLock and the anchor\n177\t        # the whole layout derives from — so the RLock that `envelope.consume` shares with `append` stays\n178\t        # the same object across a rotation (invariant 14). All DATA I/O targets `self._active` (the\n179\t        # manifest's active segment, or — with no manifest yet — the legacy single file in place, which is\n180\t        # byte-identical to the pre-rotation store).\n181\t        self.path = Path(path)\n182\t        self.path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n183\t        self._layout = SpineLayout.for_path(self.path)\n184\t        # NB: `segments/` is created only when actually migrating/rotating (not on every construction), so a\n185\t        # legacy/fresh store leaves the spine dir byte-identical to today (no stray empty dirs).\n186\t        self._manifest: Manifest | None = read_manifest(self._layout)\n187\t        self._reconcile_orphan_migration()          # complete a migrate() interrupted before the manifest write\n188\t        self._active: Path = self._resolve_active_path()\n189\t        # SEGMENT-AWARE seq -> (segment file, byte offset) index (Slice 1). Sealed segments are immutable,\n190\t        # so each is indexed once and its offsets kept forever; the active segment's tail extends on our own\n191\t        # appends (O(1)) or on the next read (over just the newly-appended bytes — offsets never move). Full\n192\t        # scans (verify/count/entries/iter_records(-1)) never touch the index, staying a single pass across\n193\t        # segments. Invalidation is EPOCH-based (`change_token`: manifest generation + active dev/ino/size),\n194\t        # so a rotation/migration is detected — a same-size in-place rewrite can no longer fool a size-only\n195\t        # heuristic (invariant 9). `_index_lock` guards the dicts against concurrent read+append.\n196\t        self._index_lock = threading.Lock()\n197\t        self._offsets: dict[int, tuple[str, int]] = {}   # seq -> (segment path str, byte offset in it)\n198\t        self._seg_scanned: dict[str, int] = {}           # segment path str -> bytes indexed so far\n199\t        self._index_epoch: tuple | None = None           # change_token when the index was last reconciled\n200\t        self._max_seq = -1\n201\t        self._last: ChainEntry | None = self._read_last_entry()\n202\t\n203\t    # --- segment layout / manifest ------------------------------------------------\n204\t    def _resolve_active_path(self) -> Path:\n205\t        \"\"\"The current append/read target. With a manifest, the active segment's absolute path; without\n206\t        one (a fresh or not-yet-migrated legacy spine), the legacy single file in place — byte-identical\n207\t        to the pre-rotation store, so nothing destructive happens until an explicit `migrate()`. A manifest\n208\t        that exists but names NO active segment is a corrupt/degenerate state and RAISES rather than\n209\t        silently falling back to `spine.jsonl` (which would resurrect a data file OUTSIDE the segment set —\n210\t        a fork; the guard also removes the same landmine for Slice-3 sealing, which must always leave an\n211\t        active segment).\"\"\"\n212\t        if self._manifest is not None:\n213\t            act = self._manifest.active()\n214\t            if act is None:\n215\t                raise SpineError(\"manifest has no active segment (corrupt/degenerate) — refusing to \"\n216\t                                 \"resurrect spine.jsonl outside the segment set\")\n217\t            return self._layout.seg_path(act)\n218\t        return self.path\n219\t\n220\t    def _read_target(self) -> Path:\n221\t        \"\"\"The file to read for THIS operation. Normally the cached active segment; if it has VANISHED (a\n222\t        migrate()/rotation in another process renamed it away), resolve the current active from the\n223\t        manifest for this read only — WITHOUT mutating shared state (lock-free reads must not race\n224\t        append's active update). Prevents a false-EMPTY read during the migrate instant, which for the\n225\t        nonce-highwater scan would regress the replay floor. In Slice 0 the only mover is migrate(), which\n226\t        RENAMES (byte-preserving), so the current index offsets stay valid for the resolved file. Fully\n227\t        epoch-invalidated, segment-spanning reads land in Slice 1.\"\"\"\n228\t        a = self._active\n229\t        if a.exists():\n230\t            return a\n231\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n232\t        act = m.active() if m is not None else None\n233\t        if act is not None:\n234\t            r = self._layout.seg_path(act)\n235\t            if r.exists():\n236\t                return r\n237\t        # PRE-manifest migrate window: the atomic rename has already placed the data at seg-0 but the\n238\t        # manifest is not published yet (so `m is None`). Read it there — otherwise a lock-free reader in\n239\t        # exactly this instant sees a false-EMPTY spine (kill-switch un-halt / nonce-floor regression) and\n240\t        # the indexed read path can hit a bare FileNotFoundError. seg-0 is byte-identical (rename), so the\n241\t        # index built over the vanished file stays valid on it.\n242\t        if m is None:\n243\t            seg0 = self._layout.segments_dir / segment_filename(0)\n244\t            if seg0.exists() and not self.path.exists():\n245\t                return seg0\n246\t        return a\n247\t\n248\t    def _segments_in_order(self) -> list[Path]:\n249\t        \"\"\"The segment files to read, in seq order (sealed by first_seq, then the active tail). With no\n250\t        manifest this is the single legacy/active file. Resolved fresh each call so a rotation/migration by\n251\t        another process is seen. RAISES SpineError on a manifest-referenced segment file that is MISSING —\n252\t        a read must never silently yield a short chain (invariant 15: no state-scanner fail-open).\"\"\"\n253\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n254\t        if m is None:\n255\t            t = self._read_target()\n256\t            return [t] if t.exists() else []\n257\t        out: list[Path] = []\n258\t        for seg in m.ordered():\n259\t            p = self._layout.seg_path(seg)\n260\t            if not p.exists():\n261\t                raise SpineError(\n262\t                    f\"spine manifest (generation {m.generation}) references a missing segment: {seg.file}\")\n263\t            out.append(p)\n264\t        return out\n265\t\n266\t    def _seam_tip_for_active(self) -> \"ChainEntry | None\":\n267\t        \"\"\"When the ACTIVE segment is empty, the chain tip its first append must extend so the seam stays\n268\t        contiguous: the prior SEALED segment's real last record (its boundary). Returns None ONLY when the\n269\t        active is the GENESIS segment (first_seq == 0) — a genuinely fresh spine correctly starts at seq 0.\n270\t        For a rotated (first_seq > 0) empty active whose seam boundary cannot be read, it FAILS CLOSED\n271\t        (raises) rather than returning None — because append treats None as 'genesis' and would otherwise\n272\t        fork the chain at seq 0 (invariant 13). The WRITE path must fail closed exactly like the read path.\n273\t        MUST be consulted under the append lock (reads the manifest fresh). No-op in Slices 0-2 (genesis).\"\"\"\n274\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n275\t        if m is None:\n276\t            return None\n277\t        act = m.active()\n278\t        if act is None or act.first_seq == 0:\n279\t            return None                              # genesis: a fresh spine correctly starts at seq 0\n280\t        sealed = m.sealed_in_order()\n281\t        if sealed:\n282\t            entry = self._entry_from_line(self._last_line_of(self._layout.seg_path(sealed[-1])))\n283\t            if entry is not None:\n284\t                return entry\n285\t        raise SpineError(\n286\t            f\"active segment first_seq={act.first_seq} is non-genesis but its seam boundary (the prior \"\n287\t            f\"sealed segment) is unreadable — refusing to fork the chain at seq 0\")\n288\t\n289\t    @contextmanager\n290\t    def _crossproc_lock(self) -> \"Iterator[None]\":\n291\t        \"\"\"Cross-process exclusion on the inode-stable lockfile (invariant 14 / D3). flock binds to the\n292\t        open file DESCRIPTION, not the path, so an os.replace of the manifest/segments cannot move the\n293\t        lock out from under a concurrent writer — which the prior flock-on-the-data-fd could not survive.\n294\t        Best-effort exactly as before (fcntl absent on non-POSIX; a flock failure is swallowed). NOT\n295\t        re-entrant across fds — never nest a second `_crossproc_lock` inside one (a second LOCK_EX on the\n296\t        same file from a different fd self-deadlocks); the seal-swap that runs inside append reuses the\n297\t        already-held lock rather than re-acquiring.\"\"\"\n298\t        if fcntl is None:  # pragma: no cover — non-POSIX\n299\t            yield\n300\t            return\n301\t        self._layout.lockfile_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n302\t        fd = os.open(str(self._layout.lockfile_path), os.O_RDWR | os.O_CREAT, 0o600)\n303\t        try:\n304\t            try:\n305\t                fcntl.flock(fd, fcntl.LOCK_EX)\n306\t            except OSError:  # pragma: no cover\n307\t                pass\n308\t            yield\n309\t        finally:\n310\t            os.close(fd)\n311\t\n312\t    def _complete_orphan_migration_locked(self) -> bool:\n313\t        \"\"\"MUST hold `_crossproc_lock`, and be called only when the manifest is currently ABSENT. If we are\n314\t        in the migrate() crash window — seg-0 present, `spine.jsonl` gone (the rename is atomic + durable),\n315\t        no manifest — finish the interrupted migration by publishing the manifest. Returns True iff it\n316\t        completed one. Called from BOTH __init__'s reconciler AND append's `_refresh_active_under_lock`, so\n317\t        a LIVE appender completes the orphan under the lock instead of re-creating `spine.jsonl` and forking\n318\t        the log (the critical review finding).\"\"\"\n319\t        seg0 = self._layout.segments_dir / segment_filename(0)\n320\t        if seg0.exists() and not self.path.exists():\n321\t            write_manifest(self._layout, initial_manifest(SCOPE, active_file=self._layout.segment_rel(0)))\n322\t            _log.warning(\"spine: completed an interrupted migration — published the manifest for %s\", seg0)\n323\t            return True\n324\t        return False\n325\t\n326\t    def _refresh_active_under_lock(self) -> None:\n327\t        \"\"\"MUST hold `_crossproc_lock`. FIRST complete any interrupted migration (so a live appender never\n328\t        resurrects `spine.jsonl`), THEN re-resolve the active segment from the manifest so an appender\n329\t        constructed before a concurrent migrate()/rotation writes to the CURRENT target — never a stale\n330\t        path (fork) or a renamed-away file (resurrection). One manifest read in the steady state.\"\"\"\n331\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n332\t        if m is None and self._complete_orphan_migration_locked():\n333\t            m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n334\t        new_active = self.path\n335\t        act = m.active() if m is not None else None\n336\t        if act is not None:\n337\t            new_active = self._layout.seg_path(act)\n338\t        changed = new_active != self._active or (m is not None) != (self._manifest is not None)\n339\t        self._manifest = m                          # keep fresh so the rotation check reuses it (no 2nd read)\n340\t        if changed:\n341\t            self._active = new_active\n342\t            self._invalidate_index()\n343\t            self._last = self._read_last_entry()\n344\t\n345\t    def _invalidate_index(self) -> None:\n346\t        \"\"\"Drops the segment-aware index so the next read rebuilds it against the (possibly changed)\n347\t        segment set.\"\"\"\n348\t        with self._index_lock:\n349\t            self._offsets = {}\n350\t            self._seg_scanned = {}\n351\t            self._index_epoch = None\n352\t            self._max_seq = -1\n353\t\n354\t    def _reconcile_orphan_migration(self) -> None:\n355\t        \"\"\"Close the migrate() crash window at CONSTRUCTION. migrate() does an ATOMIC\n356\t        `os.replace(spine.jsonl → segments/seg-00000000.jsonl)` and THEN publishes the manifest; a crash\n357\t        BETWEEN them leaves seg-0 present, `spine.jsonl` gone, and no manifest — at which point the legacy\n358\t        fallback (active = spine.jsonl) would read the spine as EMPTY and silently hide all history. If we\n359\t        observe exactly that state, finish the (already-durable) migration under the lock. (A live appender\n360\t        does the same via `_refresh_active_under_lock`; a construction-time pass just recovers faster.)\"\"\"\n361\t        if self._manifest is not None:\n362\t            return\n363\t        seg0 = self._layout.segments_dir / segment_filename(0)\n364\t        if not (seg0.exists() and not self.path.exists()):\n365\t            return\n366\t        with self._crossproc_lock():\n367\t            if read_manifest(self._layout) is None:          # re-check under the lock\n368\t                self._complete_orphan_migration_locked()\n369\t            self._manifest = read_manifest(self._layout)\n370\t\n371\t    def change_token(self) -> tuple:\n372\t        \"\"\"A cheap, rotation-aware epoch token — (manifest generation, active file, size, inode) — that\n373\t        changes on ANY append (size), a migration/rotation (generation + active path/inode), or a reset.\n374\t        THE unified replacement for the size-only 'has the spine changed?' heuristic at every site\n375\t        (invariant 9 / A4) — notably the kill-switch verdict caches in `governor/killswitch.py` and\n376\t        `gesture/session.py`. Because it reads the manifest FRESH and keys on the resolved active segment\n377\t        (not the legacy `store.path`, which a migration renames away), a same-size rotation — or a\n378\t        migration performed by another process — can never serve a stale (un-halting) kill-switch verdict.\"\"\"\n379\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n380\t        gen = m.generation if m is not None else -1\n381\t        active = self.path\n382\t        act = m.active() if m is not None else None\n383\t        if act is not None:\n384\t            active = self._layout.seg_path(act)\n385\t        try:\n386\t            st = active.stat()\n387\t            return (gen, str(active), st.st_size, st.st_ino)\n388\t        except OSError:\n389\t            return (gen, str(active), -1, -1)\n390\t\n391\t    def migrate(self) -> bool:\n392\t        \"\"\"Move a legacy single-file spine into the segment layout: rename `spine.jsonl` →\n393\t        `<stem>.segments/seg-00000000.jsonl` (atomic, same filesystem — O(1), no byte copy) and publish a\n394\t        single-active-segment manifest. Idempotent (a no-op once a manifest exists). Returns True iff it\n395\t        migrated. SPLIT-BRAIN SAFETY: run this only after every spine writer is on code that flocks the\n396\t        path-stable lockfile (this Slice-0 code) and re-resolves the active segment under that lock — a\n397\t        stale appender then completes/observes the migration instead of resurrecting `spine.jsonl`. After\n398\t        migration `spine.jsonl` is absent and is NEVER re-created as a data file (all data I/O targets the\n399\t        active segment).\"\"\"\n400\t        with spine_lock(self.path):\n401\t            with self._crossproc_lock():\n402\t                if read_manifest(self._layout) is not None:\n403\t                    self._refresh_active_under_lock()\n404\t                    return False\n405\t                self._layout.segments_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n406\t                seg0_abs = self._layout.segments_dir / segment_filename(0)\n407\t                if self.path.exists():\n408\t                    os.replace(self.path, seg0_abs)         # atomic rename (same fs); O(1), no copy\n409\t                    fsync_dir(self._layout.spine_dir)       # persist the SOURCE-dir unlink of spine.jsonl\n410\t                else:\n411\t                    seg0_abs.touch()                        # fresh spine: create the empty active segment\n412\t                fsync_dir(self._layout.segments_dir)        # persist the TARGET-dir creation of seg-0\n413\t                write_manifest(self._layout, initial_manifest(SCOPE, active_file=self._layout.segment_rel(0)))\n414\t                self._manifest = read_manifest(self._layout)\n415\t                self._active = self._resolve_active_path()\n416\t                self._invalidate_index()\n417\t                self._last = self._read_last_entry()\n418\t                return True\n419\t\n420\t    def reset(self) -> None:\n421\t        \"\"\"Delete EVERY spine artifact for this store — the legacy data file, the manifest, all segments,\n422\t        the trash, and the lockfile — for `sigil ingest --reset` (a full rebuild). Under the cross-process\n423\t        lock so it can't race an append. Idempotent; leaves the store readable (empty) afterward. Replaces\n424\t        the old `SPINE_PATH.unlink()`, which left the manifest + rotated segments behind (stale).\"\"\"\n425\t        with spine_lock(self.path):\n426\t            with self._crossproc_lock():\n427\t                # NB: do NOT unlink the lockfile — we hold its flock. flock binds to the INODE, not the\n428\t                # path (invariant 14), so unlinking it would let a concurrent cross-process appender open a\n429\t                # NEW inode at the same path and acquire the flock during our destructive rmtree — losing an\n430\t                # acked record and dangling the (re-published) manifest at a deleted segment. It is a tiny\n431\t                # 0-byte token whose entire purpose is path-stability; keep it so reset() truly excludes\n432\t                # appenders for its whole critical section.\n433\t                for pth in (self._layout.manifest_path, self.path):\n434\t                    try:\n435\t                        pth.unlink(missing_ok=True)\n436\t                    except OSError:\n437\t                        pass\n438\t                for dpath in (self._layout.segments_dir, self._layout.trash_dir):\n439\t                    if dpath.exists():\n440\t                        shutil.rmtree(dpath, ignore_errors=True)\n441\t                self._manifest = None\n442\t                self._active = self._resolve_active_path()\n443\t                self._invalidate_index()\n444\t                self._last = self._read_last_entry()\n445\t\n446\t    def generation(self) -> int:\n447\t        \"\"\"The manifest generation (monotonic rotation epoch); -1 for a legacy (un-migrated) spine.\"\"\"\n448\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n449\t        return m.generation if m is not None else -1\n450\t\n451\t    def segment_info(self) -> list[dict]:\n452\t        \"\"\"A CLI/human summary of the segment set: one dict per segment in seq order. Empty list for a\n453\t        legacy (un-migrated) single-file spine.\"\"\"\n454\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n455\t        if m is None:\n456\t            return []\n457\t        out: list[dict] = []\n458\t        for seg in m.ordered():\n459\t            p = self._layout.seg_path(seg)\n460\t            out.append({\"id\": seg.id, \"codec\": seg.codec, \"sealed\": seg.sealed,\n461\t                        \"first_seq\": seg.first_seq, \"last_seq\": seg.last_seq,\n462\t                        \"bytes\": p.stat().st_size if p.exists() else 0, \"file\": seg.file})\n463\t        return out\n464\t\n465\t    # --- rotation (retain-all seal-swap) ------------------------------------------\n466\t    def _maybe_rotate_locked(self) -> None:\n467\t        \"\"\"MUST hold the flock (called at the end of append, after the ack). Seal the active segment + start\n468\t        a fresh one if it hit a size/record threshold. Only for a MIGRATED store (a legacy single-file spine\n469\t        never auto-rotates — the owner runs `sigil spine migrate` first). Write-then-rotate: the triggering\n470\t        record is already durable in the active before we seal, so a panic append is never blocked by\n471\t        rotation for more than the bounded (O(1)+O(tail)) seal-swap (D1).\"\"\"\n472\t        if not (self._seg_max_bytes or self._seg_max_records):\n473\t            return\n474\t        m = self._manifest                          # kept fresh by _refresh_active_under_lock\n475\t        if m is None:\n476\t            return\n477\t        act = m.active()\n478\t        if act is None:\n479\t            return\n480\t        ap = self._layout.seg_path(act)\n481\t        try:\n482\t            size = ap.stat().st_size\n483\t        except OSError:\n484\t            return\n485\t        tip = self._last.seq if self._last is not None else -1\n486\t        records = (tip - act.first_seq + 1) if tip >= act.first_seq else 0\n487\t        if ((self._seg_max_bytes and size >= self._seg_max_bytes)\n488\t                or (self._seg_max_records and records >= self._seg_max_records)):\n489\t            self._seal_active_locked(m, act, ap)\n490\t\n491\t    def _seal_active_locked(self, m: Manifest, act: Segment, ap: Path) -> bool:\n492\t        \"\"\"MUST hold the flock. Seal the active segment `act` and start a fresh empty active, publishing a\n493\t        new-generation manifest ATOMICALLY (the single commit instant). Retain-all: no record is removed, so\n494\t        the signed head + verify are untouched and entry_count stays absolute. Bounded (O(1)+O(tail)).\n495\t        Ordering is crash-consistent: build the new active durably FIRST, then the manifest swap is the\n496\t        commit — a crash before it leaves the pre-rotation spine intact (the triggering record already\n497\t        durable in the old active); a crash after it leaves the post-rotation set. Returns True if sealed.\"\"\"\n498\t        # R2: seal-time torn-tail truncate (A2). The append-path truncate never revisits a sealed segment,\n499\t        # so this is the ONLY place a just-sealed file is cleaned; usually already clean (we just fsync'd).\n500\t        clean_end, boundary = _last_valid_boundary(ap)\n501\t        if boundary is None:\n502\t            return False                            # empty active — nothing to seal\n503\t        try:\n504\t            pre = ap.stat().st_size\n505\t        except OSError:\n506\t            return False\n507\t        if pre > clean_end:\n508\t            with ap.open(\"r+b\") as f:\n509\t                f.truncate(clean_end)\n510\t                f.flush()\n511\t                os.fsync(f.fileno())\n512\t        seq_hi, boundary_hash = boundary.seq, boundary.entry_hash\n513\t        new_id = act.id + 1\n514\t        new_abs = self._layout.segments_dir / segment_filename(new_id)\n515\t        # R1: GC an orphan next-segment left by a prior CRASHED seal (created but never committed to a\n516\t        # manifest). Safe: it is not in the committed segment set, so it holds no acked record.\n517\t        if new_abs.exists():\n518\t            new_abs.unlink()\n519\t        # R3: create the empty new active durably (it EXISTS before the manifest names it, so a reader that\n520\t        # sees the new manifest always finds the active — no \"active absent\" window).\n521\t        fd = os.open(str(new_abs), os.O_CREAT | os.O_EXCL | os.O_WRONLY, 0o600)\n522\t        os.fsync(fd)\n523\t        os.close(fd)\n524\t        fsync_dir(self._layout.segments_dir)\n525\t        _maybe_crash(\"seal_after_new_active\")       # crash-fuzz: new active created, manifest NOT yet swapped\n526\t        # R4: publish the new-generation manifest — the ATOMIC COMMIT INSTANT (temp→fsync→replace→dir-fsync).\n527\t        sealed = Segment(id=act.id, file=act.file, codec=act.codec, sealed=True,\n528\t                         first_seq=act.first_seq, last_seq=seq_hi, count=seq_hi - act.first_seq + 1,\n529\t                         first_prev_hash=act.first_prev_hash, boundary_hash=boundary_hash, bytes=clean_end)\n530\t        new_active = Segment(id=new_id, file=self._layout.segment_rel(new_id), codec=\"none\", sealed=False,\n531\t                             first_seq=seq_hi + 1, first_prev_hash=boundary_hash)\n532\t        others = [s for s in m.segments if s.id != act.id]\n533\t        write_manifest(self._layout, Manifest(generation=m.generation + 1, scope=m.scope,\n534\t                                              segments=others + [sealed, new_active]))\n535\t        _maybe_crash(\"seal_after_manifest\")         # crash-fuzz: committed; in-memory state not yet updated\n536\t        self._manifest = read_manifest(self._layout)\n537\t        self._active = self._layout.seg_path(new_active)\n538\t        # A seal moves NO bytes (retain-all): the just-sealed segment keeps its path + content, so its\n539\t        # indexed offsets stay valid. Do NOT clear the whole index (that would force an O(whole-spine)\n540\t        # re-scan every ~12000 records); just drop the epoch so the next read reconciles — which finds every\n541\t        # sealed segment already indexed (skip) and only registers the new empty active.\n542\t        with self._index_lock:\n543\t            self._index_epoch = None\n544\t        self._last = boundary                       # tip preserved; the next append seeds from the seam\n545\t        return True\n546\t\n547\t    def rotate(self) -> bool:\n548\t        \"\"\"Force a seal of the active segment now (for `sigil spine rotate` / an opportunistic seal at\n549\t        checkpoint). No-op on a legacy (un-migrated) or empty active. Returns True if it sealed.\"\"\"\n550\t        with spine_lock(self.path):\n551\t            with self._crossproc_lock():\n552\t                self._refresh_active_under_lock()\n553\t                m = self._manifest\n554\t                if m is None:\n555\t                    return False\n556\t                act = m.active()\n557\t                if act is None:\n558\t                    return False\n559\t                return self._seal_active_locked(m, act, self._layout.seg_path(act))\n560\t\n561\t    # --- compaction (gzip-on-seal; retain-all — no record removed) -----------------\n562\t    def compact(self) -> int:\n563\t        \"\"\"Compress every SEALED plaintext segment to gzip. Retain-all: no record is removed, and a `.gz` is\n564\t        byte-decompress-identical, so verify()/the signed head are unaffected. Reclaims ~5-8x on JSONL. The\n565\t        heavy gzip runs LOCK-FREE (a sealed segment is immutable — no append is blocked); only the tiny\n566\t        per-segment manifest codec-flip takes the flock. The superseded plaintext is MOVED to trash/ (never\n567\t        unlinked under a reader), and a reaper deletes trash older than the read-grace. Idempotent (a no-op\n568\t        on already-gzip / legacy). Returns the number compressed.\"\"\"\n569\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n570\t        if m is None:\n571\t            return 0\n572\t        n = 0\n573\t        for seg in m.sealed_in_order():\n574\t            if seg.codec == \"none\" and self._compress_one_sealed(seg.id):\n575\t                n += 1\n576\t        self._trash_orphan_plaintext()               # backstop: a crash between flip and move leaves plaintext\n577\t        self._reap_trash()\n578\t        return n\n579\t\n580\t    def _compress_one_sealed(self, seg_id: int) -> bool:\n581\t        \"\"\"P1 (lock-free gzip of the immutable sealed segment) + P2 (brief-locked manifest codec flip).\n582\t        Crash-safe: the .gz is built to a temp then atomically renamed; the codec flip is the atomic commit;\n583\t        the plaintext is left for _trash_orphan_plaintext. A crash before the flip leaves an orphan .gz that\n584\t        the next run overwrites; the plaintext (still referenced) is never at risk.\"\"\"\n585\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n586\t        seg = next((s for s in (m.segments if m else []) if s.id == seg_id and s.sealed and s.codec == \"none\"), None)\n587\t        if seg is None:\n588\t            return False\n589\t        plain = self._layout.seg_path(seg)\n590\t        if not plain.exists():\n591\t            return False\n592\t        gz_rel = self._layout.segment_rel(seg_id, \"gzip\")\n593\t        gz_abs = self._layout.spine_dir / gz_rel\n594\t        tmp = gz_abs.with_name(gz_abs.name + \".tmp\")\n595\t        with plain.open(\"rb\") as fin, gzip.open(tmp, \"wb\", compresslevel=6) as fout:\n596\t            shutil.copyfileobj(fin, fout)\n597\t        with open(tmp, \"rb\") as f:\n598\t            os.fsync(f.fileno())\n599\t        os.replace(tmp, gz_abs)\n600\t        fsync_dir(self._layout.segments_dir)\n601\t        with spine_lock(self.path):\n602\t            with self._crossproc_lock():\n603\t                m2 = read_manifest(self._layout)\n604\t                if m2 is None:\n605\t                    return False\n606\t                flipped, segs2 = False, []\n607\t                for s in m2.segments:\n608\t                    if s.id == seg_id and s.sealed and s.codec == \"none\":\n609\t                        segs2.append(s.model_copy(update={\"codec\": \"gzip\", \"file\": gz_rel,\n610\t                                                          \"bytes\": gz_abs.stat().st_size}))\n611\t                        flipped = True\n612\t                    else:\n613\t                        segs2.append(s)\n614\t                if not flipped:\n615\t                    return False                     # another process already flipped it\n616\t                write_manifest(self._layout, Manifest(generation=m2.generation + 1, scope=m2.scope, segments=segs2))\n617\t                self._manifest = read_manifest(self._layout)\n618\t                with self._index_lock:\n619\t                    self._index_epoch = None         # segment path+codec changed -> reconcile offsets\n620\t        return True\n621\t\n622\t    def _trash_orphan_plaintext(self) -> None:\n623\t        \"\"\"Move to trash any plaintext seg-k.jsonl whose manifest entry is now GZIP — i.e. the superseded\n624\t        plaintext after a codec flip (or one stranded by a crash between the flip and this move). Moving\n625\t        (not unlinking) preserves reader-grace: a reader with an open fd keeps reading, and a reader that\n626\t        ENOENTs on the vanished path re-resolves via the iter_records retry. Idempotent.\"\"\"\n627\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n628\t        if m is None:\n629\t            return\n630\t        for seg in m.segments:\n631\t            if seg.codec != \"gzip\":\n632\t                continue\n633\t            plain = self._layout.segments_dir / segment_filename(seg.id, \"none\")\n634\t            if plain.exists():\n635\t                self._layout.trash_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n636\t                try:\n637\t                    os.replace(plain, self._layout.trash_dir / f\"{plain.name}.{seg.id}\")\n638\t                except OSError:\n639\t                    pass\n640\t\n641\t    def _reap_trash(self) -> None:\n642\t        \"\"\"Delete trashed plaintext older than the read-grace (SPINE_TRASH_GRACE_SEC), which must exceed the\n643\t        longest lock-free read so no reader is ever ENOENT'd.\"\"\"\n644\t        td = self._layout.trash_dir\n645\t        if not td.exists():\n646\t            return\n647\t        cutoff = time.time() - SPINE_TRASH_GRACE_SEC\n648\t        for f in td.iterdir():\n649\t            try:\n650\t                if f.is_file() and f.stat().st_mtime < cutoff:\n651\t                    f.unlink()\n652\t            except OSError:\n653\t                pass\n654\t\n655\t    # --- write --------------------------------------------------------------------\n656\t    def append(\n657\t        self, *, kind: str, source: str, actor: str, payload: dict[str, Any],\n658\t        parent_id: int | None = None, supersedes_id: int | None = None,\n659\t        ts: str | None = None,\n660\t    ) -> int:\n661\t        content = {\n662\t            \"scope\": SCOPE, \"kind\": kind, \"source\": source, \"actor\": actor,\n663\t            \"payload\": payload, \"parent_id\": parent_id, \"supersedes_id\": supersedes_id,\n664\t        }\n665\t        cert_digest = digest_payload(content)  # wallclock-free\n666\t        # Serialize the whole read-tip → write so concurrent writers (threaded bridge server, gesture\n667\t        # daemon) can't both fork off a stale tip and break the chain. The in-process RLock is keyed on\n668\t        # the STABLE `self.path` (unchanged), so the check-then-append gate `envelope.consume` builds on\n669\t        # top of it stays atomic across a rotation. Re-read the TRUE tip from disk under the lock.\n670\t        with spine_lock(self.path):\n671\t            with self._crossproc_lock():                 # cross-process guard on the path-stable lockfile\n672\t                self._refresh_active_under_lock()        # pick up a concurrent migrate()/rotation; never fork\n673\t                active = self._active\n674\t                # binary append+read: lets us TRUNCATE a torn tail before writing (BLOCK-1 fix). `a+b`\n675\t                # creates the file if absent and — in append mode — every write still lands at EOF.\n676\t                with active.open(\"a+b\") as f:\n677\t                    # BLOCK-1: an interrupted write can leave torn bytes PAST the last valid record. Appending\n678\t                    # after them would MERGE (torn + new) into one unparseable line → the new record is silently\n679\t                    # lost, verify() stays green, and a lost kill-switch panic never halts the mesh. Truncate the\n680\t                    # dead tail back to the last valid record FIRST (this only removes never-committed garbage\n681\t                    # from an interrupted write; committed records are untouched). Evaluated on the ACTIVE\n682\t                    # segment under the lock (invariant 7 — the append target is always the true chain tip).\n683\t                    clean_end, last = _last_valid_boundary(active)\n684\t                    pre_size = os.fstat(f.fileno()).st_size\n685\t                    if pre_size > clean_end:\n686\t                        f.truncate(clean_end)\n687\t                        _log.warning(\"spine: truncated a %d-byte torn tail (interrupted write) before append (%s)\",\n688\t                                     pre_size - clean_end, active)\n689\t                    if last is not None:\n690\t                        entry = append_entry([last], cert_digest)          # extend the active segment's tip\n691\t                    else:\n692\t                        # active is EMPTY: seed from the prior sealed segment's boundary if this is a rotated\n693\t                        # (non-genesis) segment, else start the genesis chain at seq 0. Never build_chain a\n694\t                        # non-genesis segment from GENESIS (that would fork the chain at seq 0 — invariant 13).\n695\t                        seam = self._seam_tip_for_active()\n696\t                        entry = append_entry([seam], cert_digest) if seam is not None else build_chain([cert_digest])[0]\n697\t                    record = {\n698\t                        \"seq\": entry.seq, **content, \"ts\": ts or now_iso(),\n699\t                        \"cert_digest\": cert_digest, \"prev_hash\": entry.prev_hash, \"entry_hash\": entry.entry_hash,\n700\t                    }\n701\t                    line = json.dumps(record, ensure_ascii=False) + \"\\n\"\n702\t                    offset = clean_end                      # after any truncate, EOF == clean_end (where the line lands)\n703\t                    f.write(line.encode(\"utf-8\"))\n704\t                    f.flush()\n705\t                    os.fsync(f.fileno())                    # FIX 3: an ack'd append is durable across a crash\n706\t                    _maybe_crash(\"append_after_fsync\")      # crash-fuzz: record durable, not yet acked/sealed\n707\t                    # FIX 1: keep the index current with NO re-scan when the ACTIVE segment is already\n708\t                    # indexed exactly up to this record's offset. If another PROCESS appended in between,\n709\t                    # `_seg_scanned[active] < offset` and we skip — the next read's `_ensure_index` extends\n710\t                    # the active over the gap bytes AND this one. `_index_epoch` is intentionally left stale\n711\t                    # so that next read refreshes it (its extend then finds nothing to do — O(1)).\n712\t                    with self._index_lock:\n713\t                        active_str = str(active)\n714\t                        if self._seg_scanned.get(active_str) == offset:\n715\t                            self._offsets[entry.seq] = (active_str, offset)\n716\t                            if entry.seq > self._max_seq:\n717\t                                self._max_seq = entry.seq\n718\t                            self._seg_scanned[active_str] = offset + len(line.encode(\"utf-8\"))\n719\t                self._last = entry\n720\t                # write-then-rotate: the record is durable (fsync'd) above BEFORE any seal, so a panic\n721\t                # append is never blocked by rotation for more than the bounded seal-swap (D1). No-op unless\n722\t                # a migrated active hit a threshold.\n723\t                self._maybe_rotate_locked()\n724\t        return entry.seq\n725\t\n726\t    # --- read ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n727\t    def _open_segment(self, p: Path):\n728\t        \"\"\"Open a segment for a forward byte read, transparently decompressing a gzip-sealed segment. For a\n729\t        `.gz` the index stores DECOMPRESSED byte offsets, which is exactly what `gzip.GzipFile.seek()` (used\n730\t        by get()) consumes, so the offset semantics match. The active segment is always plaintext.\"\"\"\n731\t        if p.suffix == \".gz\":\n732\t            return gzip.open(p, \"rb\")\n733\t        return p.open(\"rb\")\n734\t\n735\t    def iter_records(self, *, since_seq: int = -1) -> Iterator[SpineRecord]:\n736\t        \"\"\"Records with seq > `since_seq`, in seq order, ACROSS ALL SEGMENTS (sealed segments then the\n737\t        active) as ONE contiguous chain, so every full-scanning consumer keeps seeing the whole log. For\n738\t        since_seq >= 0 the index seeks straight to the first wanted line in its owning segment\n739\t        (O(records-returned), not O(spine)); a full read (since_seq < 0) streams every segment from the\n740\t        first. A line that fails to parse or lacks required keys is SKIPPED (a torn active tail can't crash\n741\t        the read; a torn MIDDLE line becomes a seq gap that verify() fails on). RAISES SpineError if the\n742\t        manifest references a segment that cannot be read — a read must NEVER silently yield a short chain\n743\t        (invariant 15: no state-scanner fail-open).\"\"\"\n744\t        yielded_upto = since_seq\n745\t        for _attempt in range(6):                        # D2 reader-grace: tolerate a few compaction moves\n746\t            segs = self._segments_in_order()             # RAISES on a genuinely missing referenced segment\n747\t            if not segs:\n748\t                return\n749\t            start_i, start_off = self._start_locus(yielded_upto, segs)\n750\t            try:\n751\t                for i in range(start_i, len(segs)):\n752\t                    p = segs[i]\n753\t                    with self._open_segment(p) as f:\n754\t                        if i == start_i and start_off:\n755\t                            f.seek(start_off)\n756\t                        for raw in f:\n757\t                            if not raw.strip():\n758\t                                continue\n759\t                            try:\n760\t                                rec = SpineRecord.from_dict(json.loads(raw))\n761\t                            except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):\n762\t                                _log.warning(\"spine: skipping malformed line during iter_records (%s)\", p)\n763\t                                continue\n764\t                            if rec.seq > yielded_upto:\n765\t                                yield rec\n766\t                                yielded_upto = rec.seq\n767\t                return                                    # completed cleanly\n768\t            except FileNotFoundError:\n769\t                # a concurrent compaction moved a plaintext segment to trash AFTER we resolved it — re-read\n770\t                # the manifest (now pointing at the .gz) and continue from where we left off. Retain-all\n771\t                # means the same seqs are still covered, so no record is skipped or repeated.\n772\t                _log.debug(\"spine: a segment moved during read (compaction); re-resolving from seq %d\", yielded_upto)\n773\t                continue\n774\t        raise SpineError(\"spine segments kept moving during read (compaction churn) — giving up\")\n775\t\n776\t    def get(self, seq: int) -> SpineRecord | None:\n777\t        \"\"\"A single record by seq — O(1) via the segment-aware index (resolves the OWNING segment + offset),\n778\t        byte-identical to a full scan. Falls back to a bounded forward scan (which spans segments) when the\n779\t        seq is not indexed (beyond the tip, or a corrupt line was skipped).\"\"\"\n780\t        if seq < 0:\n781\t            return None\n782\t        self._ensure_index()\n783\t        with self._index_lock:\n784\t            loc = self._offsets.get(seq)\n785\t        if loc is not None:\n786\t            path_str, off = loc\n787\t            try:\n788\t                with self._open_segment(Path(path_str)) as f:\n789\t                    f.seek(off)\n790\t                    raw = f.readline()\n791\t                rec = SpineRecord.from_dict(json.loads(raw))\n792\t                if rec.seq == seq:\n793\t                    return rec\n794\t            except (OSError, ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):\n795\t                pass                                     # stale/moved offset — fall through to the scan\n796\t        for r in self.iter_records(since_seq=seq - 1):\n797\t            if r.seq == seq:\n798\t                return r\n799\t            if r.seq > seq:\n800\t                break\n801\t        return None\n802\t\n803\t    def tail(self, n: int) -> list[SpineRecord]:\n804\t        \"\"\"The last `n` records, walking segments backward from the ACTIVE across the seam (O(n) bytes,\n805\t        NOT O(spine)) — a bounded RECENT-window read on a large spine. Fewer than `n` if the spine is\n806\t        shorter. FIXES B3: the pre-Slice-1 version read only the active file, so right after a rotation it\n807\t        returned < n. NOTE (unchanged): a bounded window collapses a rapid recent replay flood but does NOT\n808\t        bound AGGREGATE replay bloat — pair `tail()`-based dedup with a record-time freshness gate.\"\"\"\n809\t        if n <= 0:\n810\t            return []\n811\t        recs: list[SpineRecord] = []\n812\t        for p in reversed(self._segments_in_order()):\n813\t            recs = self._read_segment_tail(p, n - len(recs)) + recs\n814\t            if len(recs) >= n:\n815\t                break\n816\t        return recs[-n:]\n817\t\n818\t    def _read_segment_tail(self, p: Path, k: int) -> list[SpineRecord]:\n819\t        \"\"\"The last `k` records of a single segment. Plaintext: seek-from-end (O(k) bytes). Gzip: decompress\n820\t        (bounded by the seal threshold) and take the last k lines — no random access into a gz stream.\"\"\"\n821\t        if k <= 0 or not p.exists():\n822\t            return []\n823\t        if p.suffix == \".gz\":\n824\t            with gzip.open(p, \"rb\") as f:\n825\t                lines = [x for x in f.read().split(b\"\\n\") if x.strip()]\n826\t        else:\n827\t            with p.open(\"rb\") as f:\n828\t                f.seek(0, os.SEEK_END)\n829\t                pos = f.tell()\n830\t                buf = b\"\"\n831\t                while pos > 0 and buf.count(b\"\\n\") <= k:\n832\t                    step = min(65536, pos)\n833\t                    pos -= step\n834\t                    f.seek(pos)\n835\t                    buf = f.read(step) + buf\n836\t            lines = [x for x in buf.split(b\"\\n\") if x.strip()]\n837\t        out: list[SpineRecord] = []\n838\t        for ln in lines[-k:]:\n839\t            try:\n840\t                out.append(SpineRecord.from_dict(json.loads(ln)))\n841\t            except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):\n842\t                continue\n843\t        return out\n844\t\n845\t    @property\n846\t    def next_seq(self) -> int:\n847\t        return (self._last.seq + 1) if self._last else 0\n848\t\n849\t    def count(self) -> int:\n850\t        return sum(1 for _ in self.iter_records())\n851\t\n852\t    # --- integrity ----------------------------------------------------------------\n853\t    def entries(self) -> list[ChainEntry]:\n854\t        return [\n855\t            ChainEntry(seq=r.seq, prev_hash=r.prev_hash, cert_digest=r.cert_digest, entry_hash=r.entry_hash)\n856\t            for r in self.iter_records()\n857\t        ]\n858\t\n859\t    def verify(self) -> tuple[bool, str]:\n860\t        \"\"\"Two-layer UNKEYED integrity: (1) BINDING — each record's payload still hashes to its\n861\t        stored cert_digest (catches silent payload edits); (2) CHAIN — the entries link cleanly\n862\t        (catches delete/reorder/entry tamper). This proves internal CONSISTENCY, not authenticity:\n863\t        a naive payload edit fails (1), and a mid-chain digest edit cascades an entry_hash/prev_hash\n864\t        break caught by (2) — BUT a writer who recomputes cert_digest+entry_hash for the tip (no\n865\t        successor to cascade into) or forward-cascades a fork produces a self-consistent chain that\n866\t        passes here. Resistance to a recompute-capable writer is the owner-SIGNED head's job\n867\t        (`checkpoint.verify_checkpoint`, Ed25519 + monotonic last_seq). Use this for corruption/\n868\t        naive-tamper detection; use the signed head for tamper-EVIDENCE.\"\"\"\n869\t        entries: list[ChainEntry] = []\n870\t        for r in self.iter_records():\n871\t            content = {\n872\t                \"scope\": r.scope, \"kind\": r.kind, \"source\": r.source, \"actor\": r.actor,\n873\t                \"payload\": r.payload, \"parent_id\": r.parent_id, \"supersedes_id\": r.supersedes_id,\n874\t            }\n875\t            if digest_payload(content) != r.cert_digest:\n876\t                return False, f\"binding break at seq {r.seq}: payload does not match cert_digest (record tampered)\"\n877\t            entries.append(ChainEntry(seq=r.seq, prev_hash=r.prev_hash, cert_digest=r.cert_digest, entry_hash=r.entry_hash))\n878\t        return verify_chain(entries)\n879\t\n880\t    @staticmethod\n881\t    def _entry_from_line(line: str | None) -> \"ChainEntry | None\":\n882\t        if not line:\n883\t            return None\n884\t        d = json.loads(line)\n885\t        return ChainEntry(seq=d[\"seq\"], prev_hash=d[\"prev_hash\"], cert_digest=d[\"cert_digest\"],\n886\t                          entry_hash=d[\"entry_hash\"])\n887\t\n888\t    def _last_line_of(self, p: Path) -> str | None:\n889\t        \"\"\"The last VALID record line of a segment, codec-aware. Plaintext: seek-from-end (O(tail)). Gzip:\n890\t        decompress (bounded by the seal threshold) and take the last well-formed line — used only for the\n891\t        seam boundary of an already-compressed sealed segment (a rare empty-active edge).\"\"\"\n892\t        if not p.exists():\n893\t            return None\n894\t        if p.suffix == \".gz\":\n895\t            last = None\n896\t            with gzip.open(p, \"rt\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as f:\n897\t                for line in f:\n898\t                    s = line.rstrip(\"\\n\")\n899\t                    if not s.strip():\n900\t                        continue\n901\t                    try:\n902\t                        d = json.loads(s)\n903\t                    except ValueError:\n904\t                        continue\n905\t                    if all(k in d for k in _REQUIRED_KEYS):\n906\t                        last = s\n907\t            return last\n908\t        return _last_nonempty_line(p)\n909\t\n910\t    def _read_last_entry(self) -> ChainEntry | None:\n911\t        \"\"\"The chain tip = the last valid record of the ACTIVE segment. If the active is EMPTY (a freshly\n912\t        rotated segment before its first append), the tip is the last SEALED segment's real last record\n913\t        (the seam boundary), so next_seq never regresses / reuses a seq across a rotation (invariant 13).\n914\t        Skips a torn/garbage tail, so a crash mid-write can't block a restart.\"\"\"\n915\t        m = read_manifest(self._layout)\n916\t        if m is None:\n917\t            t = self._read_target()\n918\t            return self._entry_from_line(_last_nonempty_line(t) if t.exists() else None)\n919\t        act = m.active()\n920\t        if act is not None:\n921\t            ap = self._layout.seg_path(act)\n922\t            line = _last_nonempty_line(ap) if ap.exists() else None   # active is always plaintext\n923\t            if line:\n924\t                return self._entry_from_line(line)\n925\t        for seg in reversed(m.sealed_in_order()):        # active empty/absent -> last sealed segment's tail\n926\t            line = self._last_line_of(self._layout.seg_path(seg))     # may be gz-compressed\n927\t            if line:\n928\t                return self._entry_from_line(line)\n929\t        return None\n930\t\n931\t    # --- segment-aware seq -> (segment, byte-offset) index ------------------------\n932\t    def _start_locus(self, since_seq: int, segs: list[Path]) -> tuple[int, int]:\n933\t        \"\"\"(starting segment index in `segs`, byte offset within it) so a forward read yields exactly\n934\t        seq > since_seq. A full read (since_seq < 0) → (0, 0). Otherwise the index seeks to seq\n935\t        (since_seq + 1) in its owning segment; if that seq is beyond the tip, return a past-the-end index\n936\t        so nothing is yielded; if not resolvable (below the min, or a skipped/corrupt gap), fall back to a\n937\t        safe full span from segment 0.\"\"\"\n938\t        if since_seq < 0:\n939\t            return (0, 0)\n940\t        self._ensure_index()\n941\t        want = since_seq + 1\n942\t        with self._index_lock:\n943\t            loc = self._offsets.get(want)\n944\t            max_seq = self._max_seq\n945\t        if loc is not None:\n946\t            path_str, off = loc\n947\t            for i, p in enumerate(segs):\n948\t                if str(p) == path_str:\n949\t                    return (i, off)\n950\t            return (0, 0)                           # segment set changed under us → safe full span\n951\t        if want > max_seq:\n952\t            return (len(segs), 0)                   # nothing beyond the tip → yield nothing\n953\t        return (0, 0)                               # below min / gap → safe full span\n954\t\n955\t    def _ensure_index(self) -> None:\n956\t        \"\"\"Build/reconcile the segment-aware index lazily, keyed on the epoch (`change_token`). On an epoch\n957\t        change (an append grew the active, or a rotation/migration changed the segment set) reconcile each\n958\t        segment: the ACTIVE extends over the new bytes (or re-scans if it shrank in place — a truncation);\n959\t        a SEALED segment is immutable and indexed exactly once. Thread-safe under `_index_lock`.\"\"\"\n960\t        tok = self.change_token()\n961\t        segs = self._segments_in_order()            # RAISES on a missing referenced segment, so get() (which\n962\t        #                                             calls _ensure_index) fails CLOSED too — never a\n963\t        #                                             fail-OPEN point read from a surviving segment while the\n964\t        #                                             chain is provably truncated (invariant 15).\n965\t        active_str = str(segs[-1]) if segs else None\n966\t        with self._index_lock:\n967\t            if tok == self._index_epoch:\n968\t                return\n969\t            for p in segs:\n970\t                ps = str(p)\n971\t                try:\n972\t                    size = p.stat().st_size\n973\t                except OSError:\n974\t                    continue\n975\t                scanned = self._seg_scanned.get(ps, 0)\n976\t                if ps == active_str:\n977\t                    if size > scanned:\n978\t                        self._scan_segment(ps, p, scanned, size)     # extend the active over new bytes\n979\t                    elif size < scanned:                             # active shrank in place (truncation)\n980\t                        self._purge_segment(ps)\n981\t                        self._scan_segment(ps, p, 0, size)\n982\t                elif scanned < size:                                 # sealed: immutable → index once\n983\t                    self._scan_segment(ps, p, scanned, size)\n984\t            self._index_epoch = tok\n985\t\n986\t    def _purge_segment(self, ps: str) -> None:\n987\t        \"\"\"Drop a segment's offsets (the active shrank in place — a truncation). MUST hold `_index_lock`.\"\"\"\n988\t        self._offsets = {s: loc for s, loc in self._offsets.items() if loc[0] != ps}\n989\t        self._seg_scanned.pop(ps, None)\n990\t        self._max_seq = max(self._offsets, default=-1)\n991\t\n992\t    def _scan_segment(self, ps: str, p: Path, start: int, size: int) -> None:\n993\t        \"\"\"Index every COMPLETE (newline-terminated) record line of segment `p` as\n994\t        `_offsets[seq] = (ps, line_offset)`. MUST hold `_index_lock`. Plaintext: index bytes [start, size)\n995\t        (append-only ⇒ extend-forward). Gzip (sealed, immutable): index the whole DECOMPRESSED stream once\n996\t        (offsets are decompressed byte offsets, matching `gzip.GzipFile.seek()` in get()). A malformed\n997\t        complete line is left OUT (a mid-file gap still surfaces via verify()); a trailing partial line is\n998\t        left for the next extend.\"\"\"\n999\t        if p.suffix == \".gz\":\n1000\t            with gzip.open(p, \"rb\") as gf:\n1001\t                chunk = gf.read()\n1002\t            base = 0                                    # decompressed offsets; a sealed gz is indexed once\n1003\t        else:\n1004\t            if size <= start:\n1005\t                return\n1006\t            with p.open(\"rb\") as f:\n1007\t                f.seek(start)\n1008\t                chunk = f.read(size - start)\n1009\t            base = start\n1010\t        consumed = 0\n1011\t        while True:\n1012\t            nl = chunk.find(b\"\\n\", consumed)\n1013\t            if nl == -1:\n1014\t                break                                       # trailing partial line — not complete yet\n1015\t            raw = chunk[consumed:nl]\n1016\t            line_off = base + consumed\n1017\t            consumed = nl + 1\n1018\t            if not raw.strip():\n1019\t                continue\n1020\t            try:\n1021\t                d = json.loads(raw)\n1022\t                if not all(k in d for k in _REQUIRED_KEYS):\n1023\t                    raise KeyError\n1024\t                seq = d[\"seq\"]\n1025\t            except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):\n1026\t                continue                                    # corrupt complete line — do not index it\n1027\t            self._offsets[seq] = (ps, line_off)\n1028\t            if seq > self._max_seq:\n1029\t                self._max_seq = seq\n1030\t        self._seg_scanned[ps] = base + consumed\n1031\t"}]},"uuid":"5a705c0d-772a-492b-ba3f-d5e4e48006cb","timestamp":"2026-07-19T18:54:31.412Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"360b53ff-cfe2-4bcf-9669-36ffe9c1d2cf","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"717835f0-18bc-4c20-9ce5-e0cf46c65e8e","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"8a1bd755-fe55-49e4-aa04-1c9cb10eb027","agentId":"a42954e2ff29371d9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01JYEu7ctywxgfW1hEZAmtwb","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"The spine's segment MANIFEST — the sole linearization point for the retain-all segment set.\n2\t\n3\tThe spine is stored as a sequence of size-bounded, immutable **sealed** segments plus exactly one\n4\t**active** (append) segment; the manifest names them in seq order. It is deliberately UNSIGNED and\n5\tNON-LOAD-BEARING: record order, count, and every tamper decision are re-derived from the segment BYTES\n6\t(via `verify_chain`/`classify_head`), never from the manifest's convenience fields. A doctored manifest\n7\ttherefore fails CLOSED — dropping/reordering an interior segment breaks `prev_hash`/seq contiguity, a\n8\tdropped tail shortens `entries()` (the signed head's `n < entry_count` gate fires), a dropped front fails\n9\tthe genesis check. (A future signed-manifest tier would set `manifest_sig` — reserved `null` — because it\n10\twould then attest pruned ranges; retain-all needs no such trust.)\n11\t\n12\t`SpineLayout` derives the whole on-disk layout from the spine data-file path a `SpineStore` is constructed\n13\twith, so a store on a temp path is fully isolated. This module does no locking — the caller (`SpineStore`)\n14\tholds the cross-process flock while reading/writing/migrating.\n15\t\"\"\"\n16\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n17\t\n18\tfrom dataclasses import dataclass\n19\tfrom pathlib import PurePosixPath, Path\n20\t\n21\tfrom pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict, Field, field_validator\n22\t\n23\tfrom ..reuse.chain import _GENESIS_PREV\n24\tfrom .atomicio import atomic_write_text\n25\t\n26\tSEGMENT_STEM = \"seg-\"\n27\t_SCHEMA_VERSION = 1\n28\t\n29\t\n30\tdef segment_filename(seg_id: int, codec: str = \"none\") -> str:\n31\t    \"\"\"Zero-padded segment file name; `.gz` suffix when gzip-sealed (Slice 4).\"\"\"\n32\t    base = f\"{SEGMENT_STEM}{seg_id:08d}.jsonl\"\n33\t    return base + \".gz\" if codec == \"gzip\" else base\n34\t\n35\t\n36\tclass Segment(BaseModel):\n37\t    \"\"\"One segment in the manifest. `first_prev_hash`/`boundary_hash` are the chain-linkage the reader\n38\t    CROSS-CHECKS against the segment's real first/last records — they are convenience, not authority.\"\"\"\n39\t    model_config = ConfigDict(extra=\"ignore\")   # forward-compatible: tolerate fields a newer writer added\n40\t    id: int = Field(ge=0)\n41\t    file: str                                    # path RELATIVE to the spine dir, e.g. \"segments/seg-00000000.jsonl\"\n42\t    codec: str = \"none\"                          # \"none\" | \"gzip\"\n43\t    sealed: bool = False\n44\t    first_seq: int = Field(ge=0)\n45\t    last_seq: int | None = None                  # null while active\n46\t    count: int | None = None                     # null while active; CONVENIENCE ONLY — never a tamper input\n47\t    first_prev_hash: str = _GENESIS_PREV         # == the prior segment's boundary_hash (seg 0 == genesis)\n48\t    boundary_hash: str | None = None             # == entry_hash(last_seq); null while active\n49\t    bytes: int = 0\n50\t    sha256: str | None = None                    # at-rest file integrity; NOT a chain digest\n51\t\n52\t    @field_validator(\"file\")\n53\t    @classmethod\n54\t    def _file_is_contained_relative(cls, v: str) -> str:\n55\t        \"\"\"A segment `file` must be a plain relative path under the spine dir — never absolute and never\n56\t        `..`-escaping. `get()`/`iter_records()`/`tail()` open segment files WITHOUT running verify(), so a\n57\t        doctored manifest pointing `file` at `/etc/passwd` or `../../secret` would otherwise surface that\n58\t        file's bytes AS spine records. Reject it at the model boundary so the read path stays fail-closed\n59\t        even though the manifest itself is unsigned.\"\"\"\n60\t        pp = PurePosixPath(v)\n61\t        if not v or pp.is_absolute() or PurePosixPath(v.replace(\"\\\\\", \"/\")).is_absolute() or \"..\" in pp.parts:\n62\t            raise ValueError(f\"segment file must be a contained relative path, got {v!r}\")\n63\t        return v\n64\t\n65\t\n66\tclass Manifest(BaseModel):\n67\t    model_config = ConfigDict(extra=\"ignore\")\n68\t    schema_version: int = _SCHEMA_VERSION\n69\t    generation: int = Field(default=0, ge=0)     # monotonic epoch; ++ on EVERY swap — the unified change token\n70\t    scope: str = \"\"\n71\t    segments: list[Segment] = Field(default_factory=list)\n72\t    manifest_sig: str | None = None              # reserved for the deferred signed-manifest tier\n73\t\n74\t    def active(self) -> Segment | None:\n75\t        \"\"\"The single unsealed (append) segment, if any. There is at most one.\"\"\"\n76\t        act = [s for s in self.segments if not s.sealed]\n77\t        return act[-1] if act else None\n78\t\n79\t    def sealed_in_order(self) -> list[Segment]:\n80\t        return sorted((s for s in self.segments if s.sealed), key=lambda s: s.first_seq)\n81\t\n82\t    def ordered(self) -> list[Segment]:\n83\t        \"\"\"All segments in seq order (sealed by first_seq, then the active tail).\"\"\"\n84\t        out = self.sealed_in_order()\n85\t        act = self.active()\n86\t        if act is not None:\n87\t            out.append(act)\n88\t        return out\n89\t\n90\t\n91\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n92\tclass SpineLayout:\n93\t    \"\"\"The on-disk layout derived from a spine data-file path (…/spine.jsonl). Every artifact is\n94\t    NAMESPACED BY THE DATA FILE'S STEM (`spine.manifest.json`, `spine.lock`, `spine.segments/`) so two\n95\t    stores whose data files merely SHARE a directory — e.g. the `tempfile.mktemp()` idiom that drops files\n96\t    straight into /tmp — never collide on a manifest/lock/segments set. Without this, one migrated store's\n97\t    `manifest.json` would be read by every other store in that directory and silently steer it onto the\n98\t    wrong segment (a real cross-store contamination the review caught).\"\"\"\n99\t    data_path: Path            # the legacy/identity path (…/spine.jsonl) — the stable lock/identity token\n100\t    spine_dir: Path\n101\t    segments_dir: Path\n102\t    manifest_path: Path\n103\t    lockfile_path: Path\n104\t    trash_dir: Path\n105\t\n106\t    @classmethod\n107\t    def for_path(cls, data_path: Path | str) -> \"SpineLayout\":\n108\t        p = Path(data_path)\n109\t        d = p.parent\n110\t        ns = p.stem or p.name       # \"spine.jsonl\" -> \"spine\"; namespaces every derived artifact\n111\t        return cls(\n112\t            data_path=p, spine_dir=d, segments_dir=d / f\"{ns}.segments\",\n113\t            manifest_path=d / f\"{ns}.manifest.json\", lockfile_path=d / f\"{ns}.lock\", trash_dir=d / f\"{ns}.trash\",\n114\t        )\n115\t\n116\t    def seg_path(self, seg: Segment) -> Path:\n117\t        \"\"\"Absolute path of a segment's file (its `file` is stored relative to the spine dir). The\n118\t        `Segment.file` validator guarantees it is a plain relative path that cannot escape spine_dir.\"\"\"\n119\t        return self.spine_dir / seg.file\n120\t\n121\t    def segment_rel(self, seg_id: int, codec: str = \"none\") -> str:\n122\t        \"\"\"The manifest `file` value (relative to spine_dir) for a segment id — under the namespaced\n123\t        segments dir, e.g. 'spine.segments/seg-00000000.jsonl'.\"\"\"\n124\t        return f\"{self.segments_dir.name}/{segment_filename(seg_id, codec)}\"\n125\t\n126\t\n127\tdef read_manifest(layout: SpineLayout) -> Manifest | None:\n128\t    \"\"\"Load the manifest, or None if absent. A present-but-unparseable manifest RAISES (fail closed) —\n129\t    the atomic write path guarantees it is never torn, so a parse failure means genuine corruption, not a\n130\t    partial write, and must never be silently treated as 'no manifest' (which would strand the segments).\"\"\"\n131\t    mp = layout.manifest_path\n132\t    if not mp.exists():\n133\t        return None\n134\t    return Manifest.model_validate_json(mp.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n135\t\n136\t\n137\tdef write_manifest(layout: SpineLayout, manifest: Manifest) -> None:\n138\t    \"\"\"Atomically publish the manifest (temp→fsync→os.replace→dir-fsync). THE cutover commit instant.\"\"\"\n139\t    layout.manifest_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n140\t    atomic_write_text(layout.manifest_path, manifest.model_dump_json(), prefix=\".manifest-\")\n141\t\n142\t\n143\tdef initial_manifest(scope: str, *, active_file: str, first_seq: int = 0,\n144\t                     first_prev_hash: str = _GENESIS_PREV) -> Manifest:\n145\t    \"\"\"A single-active-segment manifest (generation 0) — the shape produced by a fresh store or a\n146\t    migration of an existing single-file spine.\"\"\"\n147\t    return Manifest(\n148\t        generation=0, scope=scope,\n149\t        segments=[Segment(id=0, file=active_file, codec=\"none\", sealed=False,\n150\t                          first_seq=first_seq, first_prev_hash=first_prev_hash)],\n151\t    )\n152\t"}]},"uuid":"df83311c-8f52-43ed-b361-398eb94cfdaa","timestamp":"2026-07-19T18:56:02.788Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"717835f0-18bc-4c20-9ce5-e0cf46c65e8e","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

re-sign the spine head so the anchor sits under the spine's signature (anti-truncation)\n263:    print(f\"anchored WARDEN head into spine: count={a.count} head={a.head_hash[:16]}\")\n268:    FAILS CLOSED (exit 2) if the spine's own integrity does not hold — otherwise a surgical edit\n269:    to a warden_checkpoint line, or a spine tail-truncation removing recent anchors, would silently\n276:        print(f\"REJECTED: spine integrity failed — {msg}\", file=sys.stderr)\n278:    hok, hmsg = verify_checkpoint(store)           # signed head: catches spine tail-truncation\n280:        print(f\"REJECTED: spine head tampered — {hmsg}\", file=sys.stderr)\n345:    from .spine.store import SpineStore\n467:    print(f\"spine:     {store.count()} records | next_seq {store.next_seq}\")\n473:def cmd_spine(a) -> None:\n479:            print(\"  migrated: spine.jsonl → segments/seg-00000000.jsonl + manifest published\")\n485:    elif a.action == \"compact\":\n486:        n = store.compact()\n487:        print(f\"  compacted {n} sealed segment(s) to gzip (disk reclaimed)\" if n else \"  nothing to compact\")\n491:            print(f\"spine: LEGACY single file (not migrated) | {store.count()} records | next_seq {store.next_seq}\")\n493:        print(f\"spine: {len(segs)} segment(s) | generation {store.generation()} | \"\n523:    pi.add_argument(\"--reset\", action=\"store_true\", help=\"clear spine+cursor+vectors and rebuild\")\n602:    pwd.add_argument(\"--reason\", default=None, help=\"reason recorded on the spine\")\n613:    sub.add_parser(\"dashboard\", help=\"read-only operator status over the spine\").set_defaults(fn=cmd_dashboard)\n616:    psp = sub.add_parser(\"spine\", help=\"segment rotation: migrate the legacy file; rotate; compact (gzip); status\")\n617:    psp.add_argument(\"action\", choices=[\"migrate\", \"rotate\", \"compact\", \"status\"])\n618:    psp.set_defaults(fn=cmd_spine)\n---config diff---\ncommit 4f3c58be6e01b6f5a5a88d517869787124ac7c39\nAuthor: Water Hacker <satoshinakamotobull@gmail.com>\nDate:   Sun Jul 19 14:52:57 2026 -0400\n\n    Spine rotation Slice 4: gzip-on-seal (lock-free) + reader-grace — disk reclaimed\n    \n    This is where disk is reclaimed. Retain-all: no record removed; a .gz segment is byte-decompress-identical,\n    so verify()/the signed head are unaffected.\n    \n    - SpineStore.compact() (+ `sigil spine compact`): compress every SEALED plaintext segment to gzip. The\n      heavy gzip runs LOCK-FREE (a sealed segment is immutable — no append is blocked); only the tiny\n      per-segment manifest codec-flip takes the flock. Crash-safe: build the .gz to a temp then atomic rename;\n      the manifest codec-flip is the atomic commit; a crash before the flip leaves an orphan .gz the next run\n      overwrites (plaintext still referenced = safe); a crash after the flip leaves an orphan plaintext the\n      next run's sweep trashes. Idempotent.\n    - Reader-grace (D2): the superseded plaintext is MOVED to trash/ (never unlinked under a reader) — a reader\n      with an open fd keeps reading, and a reader that ENOENTs on the vanished path RE-RESOLVES via a new\n      iter_records manifest-reread retry (retain-all => the same seqs are still covered, none skipped/repeated).\n      A reaper deletes trash older than SPINE_TRASH_GRACE_SEC (1h default; must exceed any live read).\n    - Transparent reads: _open_segment / _scan_segment / _read_segment_tail / _last_line_of decompress a .gz\n      segment; the index stores DECOMPRESSED offsets for a .gz (matching gzip.seek in get()). The active\n      segment is always plaintext.\n    \n    Measured ~4x reclaim on a repetitive test payload (production hash-JSONL compresses ~5-8x).\n    \n    Tests (+5): compact gzips sealed + reads transparently (verify/get/tail/count/iter across gz+plaintext) +\n    idempotent; D2 reader-grace under 4 concurrent readers vs interleaved compact (no ENOENT, always a\n    contiguous chain); reap respects the grace; crash-before-flip recovers; orphan-plaintext swept after a\n    post-flip crash. 398 passed; ruff + mypy clean.\n    \n    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\n\ndiff --git a/sigil/cli.py b/sigil/cli.py\nindex ca8dc5c..fc74994 100644\n--- a/sigil/cli.py\n+++ b/sigil/cli.py\n@@ -482,6 +482,9 @@ def cmd_spine(a) -> None:\n     elif a.action == \"rotate\":\n         print(\"  sealed the active segment + started a new one\" if store.rotate()\n               else \"  nothing to rotate (legacy or empty active)\")\n+    elif a.action == \"compact\":\n+        n = store.compact()\n+        print(f\"  compacted {n} sealed segment(s) to gzip (disk reclaimed)\" if n else \"  nothing to compact\")\n     elif a.action == \"status\":\n         segs = store.segment_info()\n         if not segs:\n@@ -610,8 +613,8 @@ def main(argv=None) -> None:\n     sub.add_parser(\"dashboard\", help=\"read-only operator status over the spine\").set_defaults(fn=cmd_dashboard)\n     sub.add_parser(\"verify\").set_defaults(fn=cmd_verify)\n     sub.add_parser(\"status\").set_defaults(fn=cmd_status)\n-    psp = sub.add_parser(\"spine\", help=\"segment rotation: migrate the legacy file into segments; rotate; status\")\n-    psp.add_argument(\"action\", choices=[\"migrate\", \"rotate\", \"status\"])\n+    psp = sub.add_parser(\"spine\", help=\"segment rotation: migrate the legacy file; rotate; compact (gzip); status\")\n+    psp.add_argument(\"action\", choices=[\"migrate\", \"rotate\", \"compact\", \"status\"])\n     psp.set_defaults(fn=cmd_spine)\n     psv = sub.add_parser(\"serve\", help=\"start the loopback glass-cockpit UI\")\n     psv.add_argument(\"--port\", type=int, default=8733)\ndiff --git a/sigil/config.py b/sigil/config.py\nindex 82e446e..57b51a7 100644\n--- a/sigil/config.py\n+++ b/sigil/config.py\n@@ -72,6 +72,9 @@ def _int_env(name: str, default: int) -> int:\n \n SPINE_SEG_MAX_BYTES = _int_env(\"SIGIL_SPINE_SEG_MAX_BYTES\", 16 * 1024 * 1024)   # 16 MiB\n SPINE_SEG_MAX_RECORDS = _int_env(\"SIGIL_SPINE_SEG_MAX_RECORDS\", 12_000)\n+# Grace before a superseded (compressed-away) plaintext segment in trash/ is reaped: must exceed the\n+# longest lock-free read, so a reader mid-scan is never ENOENT'd. Any bounded read is sub-second; 1h is safe.\n+SPINE_TRASH_GRACE_SEC = _int_env(\"SIGIL_SPINE_TRASH_GRACE_SEC\", 3600)\n \n # --- ingestion sources ----------------------------------------------------------------\n CLAUDE_PROJECTS = Path(os.environ.get(\"SIGIL_CLAUDE_PROJECTS\", str(Path.home() / \".claude\" / \"projects\")))","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"860d35df-337c-49be-a460-b80d13a0e526","timestamp":"2026-07-19T18:56:21.560Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"6538de13-d7e3-43b7-94d4-9e3415f65ea3","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"3da20619-116c-4265-bd72-c871e352ef00","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"8a1bd755-fe55-49e4-aa04-1c9cb10eb027","agentId":"a42954e2ff29371d9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01CTnJARXpNDhXcsLAMBBd6H","type":"tool_result","content":"\"\"\"SIGIL spine segment rotation — Slice 0: manifest + path-stable lockfile + split-brain-safe migration.\n\nSlice 0 introduces the layout (manifest.json, spine.lock, segments/) and an EXPLICIT `migrate()`, but\nkeeps behavior byte-identical until then: with no manifest the store reads/writes the legacy single file\nin place. These tests pin the Slice-0 invariants:\n  - a legacy store is byte-identical (no manifest / segments dir created; data stays in spine.jsonl);\n  - the in-process RLock is keyed on the STABLE store.path and survives a migration (invariant 14 — the\n    lock `envelope.consume` shares with `append` is the same object across a rotation);\n  - migrate() renames spine.jsonl → segments/seg-00000000.jsonl (O(1)) + publishes a manifest, preserving\n    the chain, next_seq, and verify();\n  - a STALE appender constructed before a concurrent migrate() re-resolves the active segment under the\n    lock and never resurrects spine.jsonl (the split-brain the two-step migration guards against).\nRun: ~/.sigil/venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_spine_rotation.py -q\n\"\"\"\nimport gzip\nimport json\nimport os\nimport shutil\nimport tempfile\nimport threading\nimport time\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom sigil.reuse.chain import _GENESIS_PREV\nfrom sigil.spine.manifest import Manifest, Segment, read_manifest, write_manifest, SpineLayout\nfrom sigil.spine.store import SpineError, SpineStore, spine_lock\n\n\ndef _fresh_dir(prefix: str) -> Path:\n    return Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix=prefix))\n\n\ndef _append_n(store: SpineStore, n: int, start: int = 0) -> None:\n    for i in range(start, start + n):\n        store.append(kind=\"event\", source=\"t\", actor=\"u\", payload={\"n\": i})\n\n\ndef _make_segmented(d: Path, seg_counts: list[int]) -> SpineLayout:\n    \"\"\"Build a real chain of sum(seg_counts) records, then physically lay it out as len(seg_counts)\n    segments (the last is the ACTIVE) with a valid manifest — a genuine multi-segment spine of exactly the\n    shape Slice 3's rotation will produce. The chain spans every seam (each segment's first record's\n    prev_hash == the prior segment's last entry_hash), because the records were ONE chain before the split.\"\"\"\n    p = d / \"spine.jsonl\"\n    s = SpineStore(p)\n    _append_n(s, sum(seg_counts))\n    lay = SpineLayout.for_path(p)\n    lines = [ln for ln in p.read_bytes().splitlines(keepends=True) if ln.strip()]\n    recs = [json.loads(ln) for ln in lines]\n    assert len(recs) == sum(seg_counts)\n    lay.segments_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    segments, idx, prev_boundary, prev_seq = [], 0, _GENESIS_PREV, -1\n    for seg_id, cnt in enumerate(seg_counts):\n        seg_lines, seg_recs = lines[idx:idx + cnt], recs[idx:idx + cnt]\n        (lay.segments_dir / f\"seg-{seg_id:08d}.jsonl\").write_bytes(b\"\".join(seg_lines))\n        is_last = seg_id == len(seg_counts) - 1\n        first_seq = seg_recs[0][\"seq\"] if seg_recs else prev_seq + 1   # empty active -> next seq after boundary\n        segments.append(Segment(\n            id=seg_id, file=f\"{lay.segments_dir.name}/seg-{seg_id:08d}.jsonl\", codec=\"none\",\n            sealed=not is_last, first_seq=first_seq,\n            last_seq=None if is_last else seg_recs[-1][\"seq\"],\n            count=None if is_last else cnt,\n            first_prev_hash=prev_boundary,\n            boundary_hash=None if is_last else seg_recs[-1][\"entry_hash\"],\n        ))\n        if seg_recs:\n            prev_boundary, prev_seq = seg_recs[-1][\"entry_hash\"], seg_recs[-1][\"seq\"]\n        idx += cnt\n    p.unlink()                                             # the legacy file is now split into segments\n    write_manifest(lay, Manifest(generation=1, scope=\"\", segments=segments))\n    return lay","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"d41e10c7-e0eb-4103-9f74-2d8843008782","timestamp":"2026-07-19T18:56:30.009Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"3da20619-116c-4265-bd72-c871e352ef00","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}
