{"parentUuid":"273edd99-96b2-4310-a4cd-4d8deb85fbd0","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"8a1bd755-fe55-49e4-aa04-1c9cb10eb027","agentId":"a42954e2ff29371d9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01YagCeMU8pExF1mcNuqSLuc","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"Daily signed checkpoint of the spine head (SIGIL §6.3.6, WARDEN anchor).\n2\t\n3\tGenerates + persists a solo-owner Ed25519 keypair once (1-of-1 trust root), then signs\n4\tthe chain head so the whole spine is anchored: rewriting history would require forging\n5\tthe owner signature, and a shrunk/back-dated head is caught by the monotonic last_seq.\n6\t\n7\tNote: for a personal, single-owner, local-first system the owner's signing key lives\n8\tlocally (0600) — a deliberate simplification of the framework's \"keys never on the\n9\truntime host\" rule, appropriate to this threat model (§1.3). A hardware co-signer can be\n10\tadded later by raising the trust-root threshold.\n11\t\"\"\"\n12\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n13\t\n14\timport os\n15\tfrom pathlib import Path\n16\t\n17\tfrom ..config import HEAD_PATH, KEYS_DIR, OWNER_KEY_ID, SCOPE\n18\tfrom ..reuse import (\n19\t    AuthorizerKey,\n20\t    SignedChainHead,\n21\t    TrustRoot,\n22\t    generate_keypair,\n23\t    sign_head,\n24\t    verify_head,\n25\t)\n26\tfrom .atomicio import atomic_write_text\n27\tfrom .store import SpineStore\n28\t\n29\t_PRIV = KEYS_DIR / \"owner.priv\"\n30\t_PUB = KEYS_DIR / \"owner.pub\"\n31\t\n32\t\n33\tdef _atomic_write_text(path: Path, data: str) -> None:\n34\t    \"\"\"Durably + atomically replace `path` with `data` (FIX 3) — a reader sees either the whole old or\n35\t    the whole new head, never a torn one, and a crash leaves the previous valid head intact. Delegates to\n36\t    the one shared `spine.atomicio.atomic_write_text` so the head, the manifest, and every cutover use the\n37\t    same audited routine (no drift).\"\"\"\n38\t    atomic_write_text(path, data, prefix=\".head-\")\n39\t\n40\t\n41\tdef _owner_keys() -> tuple[str, str]:\n42\t    if not _PRIV.exists():\n43\t        KEYS_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n44\t        kp = generate_keypair()\n45\t        _PRIV.write_text(kp.private_key_b64, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n46\t        os.chmod(_PRIV, 0o600)\n47\t        _PUB.write_text(kp.public_key_b64, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n48\t    return _PRIV.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\").strip(), _PUB.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\").strip()\n49\t\n50\t\n51\tdef trust_root() -> TrustRoot:\n52\t    _, pub = _owner_keys()\n53\t    return TrustRoot(threshold=1, authorizers=[\n54\t        AuthorizerKey(key_id=OWNER_KEY_ID, name=\"owner\", public_key_b64=pub)])\n55\t\n56\t\n57\tdef checkpoint(store: SpineStore | None = None) -> SignedChainHead:\n58\t    store = store or SpineStore()\n59\t    priv, _ = _owner_keys()\n60\t    head = sign_head(store.entries(), engagement_slug=SCOPE, signers=[(OWNER_KEY_ID, priv)])\n61\t    _atomic_write_text(HEAD_PATH, head.model_dump_json())   # FIX 3: never leave a torn head on a crash\n62\t    return head\n63\t\n64\t\n65\tdef classify_head(head: SignedChainHead, entries: list, tr: TrustRoot) -> tuple[bool, str]:\n66\t    \"\"\"Pure head/chain classification (no globals — testable in isolation). Distinguishes\n67\t    three cases honestly: a shrunk chain (truncation) or a mismatching prefix (history\n68\t    rewrite) are TAMPERING; a chain that merely grew past the anchor is benign — stale.\n69\t    \"\"\"\n70\t    n, signed_n = len(entries), head.entry_count\n71\t    if n < signed_n:\n72\t        return False, f\"TAMPERING: chain has {n} records but the signed head anchors {signed_n} (truncated/rolled back)\"\n73\t    # verify the head against exactly the prefix it signed; append-only ⇒ the prefix is byte-identical.\n74\t    ok, msg = verify_head(head, entries[:signed_n], tr)\n75\t    if not ok:\n76\t        return False, f\"TAMPERING: history rewritten at or below the signed head — {msg}\"\n77\t    if n > signed_n:\n78\t        return True, f\"anchors {signed_n} records; {n - signed_n} appended since — run `sigil sign` to re-anchor\"\n79\t    return True, f\"anchors all {signed_n} records (current)\"\n80\t\n81\t\n82\tdef verify_checkpoint(store: SpineStore | None = None) -> tuple[bool, str]:\n83\t    store = store or SpineStore()\n84\t    if not HEAD_PATH.exists():\n85\t        return False, \"no signed head — run `sigil sign` to anchor the spine\"\n86\t    head = SignedChainHead.model_validate_json(HEAD_PATH.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n87\t    return classify_head(head, store.entries(), trust_root())\n88\t"}]},"uuid":"e9e3f63d-54e0-48f9-8439-de402f692732","timestamp":"2026-07-19T18:56:44.618Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"273edd99-96b2-4310-a4cd-4d8deb85fbd0","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

across compact?\")\nprint(\"  equal =\", ref == after, \" len_before=\", len(ref), \" len_after=\", len(after))\nbanner(\"verify after compact\")\nprint(\" \", s.verify(), \" count=\", s.count(), \" p = d/\"spine.jsonl\"\ns = SpineStore(p, seg_max_bytes=0, seg_max_records=4)\ns.migrate()\nfor i in range(30): s.append(kind=\"event\", source=\"t\", actor=\"u\", payload={\"n\": i})\ns.compact()\nref = {r.seq: r.entry_hash for r in SpineStore(p, seg_max_records=4).iter_records()}\nbanner(\"iter_records(since_seq=k) landing mid-gz (seek+iterate on GzipFile)\")\nbad=[]\nfor since in range(-1, 30):\n    fresh = SpineStore(p, seg_max_records=4)   # fresh instance each time\n    got = [(r.seq, r.entry_hash) for r in fresh.iter_records(since_seq=since)]\n    exp = [(seq, ref[seq]) for seq in sorted(ref) if seq > since]\n    if got != exp:\n        bad.append((since, [g[0] for g in got], [e[0] for e in exp]))\nprint(\"  mismatches:\", bad if bad else \"NONE\")\n\n# ---------- cross-process: store A reads (builds index), store B compacts, store A reads again ----------\nbanner(\"stale long-lived store re-reads after ANOTHER store compacts\")\nd2 = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix=\"p2b-\")); p2 = d2/\"spine.jsonl\"\nA = SpineStore(p2, seg_max_records=4); A.migrate()\nfor i in range(20): A.append(kind=\"event\", source=\"t\", actor=\"u\", payload={\"n\": i})\n_ = list(A.iter_records())            # A builds its index over PLAINTEXT segments\ng_before = A.get(5).entry_hash\nB = SpineStore(p2, seg_max_records=4) # separate instance\nnB = B.compact()\nprint(\"  B.compact() ->\", nB)\n# A still has stale index pointing at plaintext paths (now moved to trash)\ntry:\n    a_iter = [r.seq for r in A.iter_records()]\n    print(\"  A.iter_records after B compact -> len\", len(a_iter), \"contiguous\", a_iter==list(range(20)))\nexcept Exception as e:\n    print(\"  A.iter_records RAISED:\", type(e).__name__, e)\ntry:\n    g_after = A.get(5)\n    print(\"  A.get(5) after B compact ->\", None if g_after is None else (g_after.seq, g_after.entry_hash==g_before))\nexcept Exception as e:\n    print(\"  A.get(5) RAISED:\", type(e).__name__, e)\n\n# ---------- incompressible / tiny gz: does reconcile RE-SCAN every epoch? correctness? ----------\nbanner(\"incompressible sealed segment: decompressed < compressed -> re-scan branch\")\nimport sigil.spine.store as store_mod\nd3 = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix=\"p2c-\")); p3 = d3/\"spine.jsonl\"\ns3 = SpineStore(p3, seg_max_records=1)  # seal EVERY record -> tiny 1-record segments\ns3.migrate()\nfor i in range(6): s3.append(kind=\"event\", source=\"t\", actor=\"u\", payload={\"n\": i})\ns3.compact()\nm3 = read_manifest(s3._layout)\nfor seg in m3.sealed_in_order():\n    ap = s3._layout.seg_path(seg)\n    dec = len(gzip.open(ap,\"rb\").read()); comp = ap.stat().st_size\n    print(f\"  seg {seg.id}: compressed={comp} decompressed={dec} incompressible={dec<comp}\")\n# instrument _scan_segment call count over repeated get()/appends\ncalls = {\"n\":0}\norig = store_mod.SpineStore._scan_segment\ndef counting(self, ps, p, start, size):\n    if str(p).endswith(\".gz\"): calls[\"n\"] += 1\n    return orig(self, ps, p, start, size)\nstore_mod.SpineStore._scan_segment = counting\nsX = SpineStore(p3, seg_max_records=1)\nsX.get(0)                      # first index build: scans each gz once\nbase = calls[\"n\"]; print(\"  gz scans on first index build:\", base)\nfor i in range(6, 12):         # each append changes epoch -> reconcile\n    sX.append(kind=\"event\", source=\"t\", actor=\"u\", payload={\"n\": i})\n    sX.get(0)\nprint(\"  gz scans after 6 more appends+reads (re-scan churn):\", calls[\"n\"]-base, \"(0 == indexed-once, >0 == re-scan every epoch)\")\nstore_mod.SpineStore._scan_segment = orig\n# correctness after churn\nfull = {r.seq:r.entry_hash for r in sX.iter_records()}\nmm=[seq for seq in range(6) if sX.get(seq).entry_hash != full[seq]]\nprint(\"  correctness after churn: mismatches\", mm if mm else \"NONE\")\n\n# ---------- empty active + last sealed is GZ: next_seq / seam via _last_line_of(gz) ----------\nbanner(\"empty active whose seam boundary is a GZ sealed segment\")\nd4 = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix=\"p2d-\")); p4 = d4/\"spine.jsonl\"\ns4 = SpineStore(p4, seg_max_records=4); s4.migrate()\nfor i in range(8): s4.append(kind=\"event\", source=\"t\", actor=\"u\", payload={\"n\": i})\ns4.rotate()  # force seal -> active becomes empty (first_seq=8)\ns4.compact() # gzip the sealed incl the one right before the empty active\nm4 = read_manifest(s4._layout)\nprint(\"  segments:\", [(sg.id, sg.codec, sg.sealed, sg.first_seq, sg.last_seq) for sg in m4.ordered()])\nfresh = SpineStore(p4, seg_max_records=4)\nprint(\"  next_seq (expect 8):\", fresh.next_seq)\nsn = fresh.append(kind=\"event\", source=\"t\", actor=\"u\", payload={\"n\": 99})\nprint(\"  appended seq (expect 8):\", sn)\nprint(\"  verify after seam-append over gz:\", fresh.verify())\nprint(\"\\nDONE\")\nPYEOF\ncd /home/kali/sigil && ~/.sigil/venv/bin/python /tmp/claude-1000/-home-kali-Pictures-PENTEST-main/7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27/scratchpad/probe2.py 2>&1","description":"Probe seek+iterate, stale index, incompressible rescan, gz seam"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":3273,"cache_read_input_tokens":89442,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":3273,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CdBvnxsS8DppoPbAermZs","attributionAgent":"general-purpose","type":"assistant","uuid":"3c3eef06-5607-4c39-b5c3-0253b1dde7a6","timestamp":"2026-07-19T18:59:23.682Z","effort":"xhigh","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"eeccc282-f960-4975-9421-b18f5c5c9f73","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"8a1bd755-fe55-49e4-aa04-1c9cb10eb027","agentId":"a42954e2ff29371d9","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_015r1qMew7yqWqo2HRA7YNtR","type":"tool_result","content":"commit 4f3c58be6e01b6f5a5a88d517869787124ac7c39\nAuthor: Water Hacker <satoshinakamotobull@gmail.com>\nDate:   Sun Jul 19 14:52:57 2026 -0400\n\n    Spine rotation Slice 4: gzip-on-seal (lock-free) + reader-grace — disk reclaimed\n    \n    This is where disk is reclaimed. Retain-all: no record removed; a .gz segment is byte-decompress-identical,\n    so verify()/the signed head are unaffected.\n    \n    - SpineStore.compact() (+ `sigil spine compact`): compress every SEALED plaintext segment to gzip. The\n      heavy gzip runs LOCK-FREE (a sealed segment is immutable — no append is blocked); only the tiny\n      per-segment manifest codec-flip takes the flock. Crash-safe: build the .gz to a temp then atomic rename;\n      the manifest codec-flip is the atomic commit; a crash before the flip leaves an orphan .gz the next run\n      overwrites (plaintext still referenced = safe); a crash after the flip leaves an orphan plaintext the\n      next run's sweep trashes. Idempotent.\n    - Reader-grace (D2): the superseded plaintext is MOVED to trash/ (never unlinked under a reader) — a reader\n      with an open fd keeps reading, and a reader that ENOENTs on the vanished path RE-RESOLVES via a new\n      iter_records manifest-reread retry (retain-all => the same seqs are still covered, none skipped/repeated).\n      A reaper deletes trash older than SPINE_TRASH_GRACE_SEC (1h default; must exceed any live read).\n    - Transparent reads: _open_segment / _scan_segment / _read_segment_tail / _last_line_of decompress a .gz\n      segment; the index stores DECOMPRESSED offsets for a .gz (matching gzip.seek in get()). The active\n      segment is always plaintext.\n    \n    Measured ~4x reclaim on a repetitive test payload (production hash-JSONL compresses ~5-8x).\n    \n    Tests (+5): compact gzips sealed + reads transparently (verify/get/tail/count/iter across gz+plaintext) +\n    idempotent; D2 reader-grace under 4 concurrent readers vs interleaved compact (no ENOENT, always a\n    contiguous chain); reap respects the grace; crash-before-flip recovers; orphan-plaintext swept after a\n    post-flip crash. 398 passed; ruff + mypy clean.\n    \n    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\n\ndiff --git a/tests/test_spine_rotation.py b/tests/test_spine_rotation.py\nindex 99e8195..5f8dc36 100644\n--- a/tests/test_spine_rotation.py\n+++ b/tests/test_spine_rotation.py\n@@ -12,9 +12,13 @@ in place. These tests pin the Slice-0 invariants:\n     lock and never resurrects spine.jsonl (the split-brain the two-step migration guards against).\n Run: ~/.sigil/venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_spine_rotation.py -q\n \"\"\"\n+import gzip\n import json\n import os\n+import shutil\n import tempfile\n+import threading\n+import time\n from pathlib import Path\n \n from sigil.reuse.chain import _GENESIS_PREV\n@@ -475,6 +479,132 @@ def test_panic_append_durable_when_it_triggers_rotation():\n     assert ok, reason\n \n \n+def test_compact_gzips_sealed_and_reads_transparently():\n+    d = _fresh_dir(\"sigil-compact-\")\n+    p = d / \"spine.jsonl\"\n+    lay = SpineLayout.for_path(p)\n+    s = SpineStore(p, seg_max_bytes=0, seg_max_records=40)\n+    s.migrate()\n+    for i in range(180):\n+        s.append(kind=\"event\", source=\"t\", actor=\"a\", payload={\"n\": i, \"text\": \"lorem ipsum dolor sit amet \" * 3})\n+    before = sum(f.stat().st_size for f in lay.segments_dir.glob(\"*.jsonl\"))\n+    n = s.compact()\n+    assert n >= 3, f\"expected several sealed segments compressed, got {n}\"\n+    after = sum(f.stat().st_size for f in lay.segments_dir.glob(\"*.gz\"))\n+    assert after < before, f\"gzip must reclaim disk: {after} !< {before}\"\n+    codecs = {x[\"id\"]: x[\"codec\"] for x in s.segment_info()}\n+    assert all(codecs[i] == \"gzip\" for i in codecs if i != max(codecs)), \"all sealed segments gzip\"\n+    assert codecs[max(codecs)] == \"none\", \"active segment stays plaintext\"\n+\n+    # every read is transparent across gz + plaintext\n+    fresh = SpineStore(p)\n+    ok, reason = fresh.verify()\n+    assert ok, reason\n+    assert [r.seq for r in fresh.iter_records()] == list(range(180))\n+    assert fresh.count() == 180 and fresh.next_seq == 180\n+    for seq in (0, 41, 179):                                # get() from a gz segment and the active\n+        assert fresh.get(seq).payload[\"n\"] == seq\n+    assert [r.seq for r in fresh.tail(3)] == [177, 178, 179]\n+    assert s.compact() == 0, \"idempotent — nothing left to compact\"\n+    # a post-compact append still verifies and rotation still works over gz segments\n+    s.append(kind=\"event\", source=\"t\", actor=\"a\", payload={\"n\": 180})\n+    ok, reason = SpineStore(p).verify()\n+    assert ok, reason\n+\n+\n+def test_reader_grace_during_concurrent_compact():\n+    \"\"\"D2: lock-free readers iterating while a compactor gzips sealed segments (moving superseded plaintext\n+    to trash) must NEVER ENOENT or see a short/forked chain — the manifest-reread retry + open-fd grace\n+    keep every read a contiguous chain from genesis.\"\"\"\n+    d = _fresh_dir(\"sigil-d2-\")\n+    p = d / \"spine.jsonl\"\n+    s = SpineStore(p, seg_max_bytes=0, seg_max_records=15)\n+    s.migrate()\n+    _append_n(s, 60)\n+\n+    errors: list[str] = []\n+    stop = threading.Event()\n+\n+    def reader():\n+        r = SpineStore(p)\n+        while not stop.is_set():\n+            try:\n+                seqs = [rec.seq for rec in r.iter_records()]\n+            except Exception as e:                          # noqa: BLE001 — any read exception is a failure\n+                errors.append(f\"reader raised {e!r}\")\n+                return\n+            if seqs != list(range(len(seqs))):\n+                errors.append(f\"reader saw a non-contiguous chain: {seqs[:3]}..{seqs[-3:]}\")\n+                return\n+\n+    threads = [threading.Thread(target=reader) for _ in range(4)]\n+    for t in threads:\n+        t.start()\n+    for k in range(4):                                      # interleave: more sealed plaintext, then compact\n+        _append_n(s, 15, start=60 + k * 15)\n+        s.compact()\n+        time.sleep(0.005)\n+    stop.set()\n+    for t in threads:\n+        t.join(timeout=10)\n+    assert not errors, errors[:3]\n+\n+\n+def test_reap_trash_respects_grace(monkeypatch):\n+    import sigil.spine.store as store_mod\n+\n+    d = _fresh_dir(\"sigil-reap-\")\n+    p = d / \"spine.jsonl\"\n+    lay = SpineLayout.for_path(p)\n+    s = SpineStore(p, seg_max_bytes=0, seg_max_records=5)\n+    s.migrate()\n+    _append_n(s, 12)\n+    s.compact()\n+    assert list(lay.trash_dir.iterdir()), \"compact trashes superseded plaintext\"\n+    s._reap_trash()                                         # default grace (1h) => young trash survives\n+    assert list(lay.trash_dir.iterdir()), \"trash within the read-grace is NOT reaped\"\n+    monkeypatch.setattr(store_mod, \"SPINE_TRASH_GRACE_SEC\", -1)\n+    s._reap_trash()                                         # grace -1 => everything is past grace\n+    assert not list(lay.trash_dir.iterdir()), \"trash past the grace is reaped\"\n+\n+\n+def test_compact_recovers_from_crash_before_codec_flip():\n+    \"\"\"Crash after building the .gz but before the manifest flip: an orphan .gz, manifest still plaintext.\n+    Reads use the plaintext; re-running compact overwrites the orphan and completes.\"\"\"\n+    d = _fresh_dir(\"sigil-ccrash1-\")\n+    p = d / \"spine.jsonl\"\n+    lay = SpineLayout.for_path(p)\n+    s = SpineStore(p, seg_max_bytes=0, seg_max_records=5)\n+    s.migrate()\n+    _append_n(s, 12)\n+    plain0 = lay.segments_dir / \"seg-00000000.jsonl\"\n+    with plain0.open(\"rb\") as fin, gzip.open(lay.segments_dir / \"seg-00000000.jsonl.gz\", \"wb\") as fout:\n+        shutil.copyfileobj(fin, fout)                       # simulate the orphan .gz (crashed before flip)\n+    ok, reason = SpineStore(p).verify()\n+    assert ok, reason                                       # manifest still plaintext -> reads use it\n+    s.compact()\n+    assert s.segment_info()[0][\"codec\"] == \"gzip\"\n+    ok, reason = SpineStore(p).verify()\n+    assert ok, reason\n+\n+\n+def test_compact_sweeps_orphan_plaintext_after_flip():\n+    \"\"\"Crash after the codec flip but before the plaintext move: an orphan plaintext beside the gz. The\n+    next compact()'s orphan-sweep trashes it.\"\"\"\n+    d = _fresh_dir(\"sigil-ccrash2-\")\n+    p = d / \"spine.jsonl\"\n+    lay = SpineLayout.for_path(p)\n+    s = SpineStore(p, seg_max_bytes=0, seg_max_records=5)\n+    s.migrate()\n+    _append_n(s, 12)\n+    s.compact()                                            # seg-0 -> gzip, plaintext trashed\n+    (lay.segments_dir / \"seg-00000000.jsonl\").write_bytes(b'{\"stale\":true}\\n')   # crash-stranded plaintext\n+    s.compact()                                            # orphan-sweep must trash it\n+    assert not (lay.segments_dir / \"seg-00000000.jsonl\").exists(), \"orphan plaintext swept to trash\"\n+    ok, reason = SpineStore(p).verify()\n+    assert ok, reason\n+\n+\n def test_reset_keeps_lockfile_so_it_still_excludes(monkeypatch):\n     \"\"\"Review F1: reset() must NOT unlink the lockfile it holds (flock binds to the inode, not the path),\n     or a concurrent appender opens a new inode and slips the lock during the destructive rmtree. a.write_text(\"x\")\nold = time.time() - 7200  # 2h ago\nos.utime(a, (old, old))\nb = dd/\"b\"\nos.replace(a, b)\nprint(f\"  source mtime set 2h ago={old:.0f}; after rename dest mtime={b.stat().st_mtime:.0f}  preserved={abs(b.stat().st_mtime-old)<1}\")\n\n# 2) Realistic spine: segments sealed >1h ago, THEN compact()\nbanner(\"realistic aged spine: does compact() immediately reap the plaintext it just trashed?\")\nd = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix=\"p3-\")); p = d/\"spine.jsonl\"\ns = SpineStore(p, seg_max_bytes=0, seg_max_records=5)\ns.migrate()\nfor i in range(22): s.append(kind=\"event\", source=\"t\", actor=\"u\", payload={\"n\": i})\nlay = s._layout\n# simulate that these sealed segments were written well in the past (a spine that grew over days;\n# compaction is a periodic maintenance op run long after the segments were sealed)\nold = time.time() - 3*3600   # 3h ago  (grace is 1h)\nfor f in lay.segments_dir.glob(\"seg-*.jsonl\"):\n    os.utime(f, (old, old))\nprint(\"  sealed plaintext files pre-compact:\", sorted(x.name for x in lay.segments_dir.glob('seg-*.jsonl')))\nn = s.compact()\nprint(\"  compact() compressed:\", n)\ntrash = sorted(lay.trash_dir.iterdir()) if lay.trash_dir.exists() else []\nprint(\"  TRASH after compact():\", [t.name for t in trash])\nprint(\"  --> grace window for the just-superseded plaintext:\",\n      \"ZERO (reaped in the same compact call)\" if not trash else \"preserved\")\n\n# 3) What SHOULD happen with a young spine (the test's scenario)\nbanner(\"young spine (test scenario): trash survives\")\nd2 = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix=\"p3b-\")); p2 = d2/\"spine.jsonl\"\ns2 = SpineStore(p2, seg_max_bytes=0, seg_max_records=5); s2.migrate()\nfor i in range(22): s2.append(kind=\"event\", source=\"t\", actor=\"u\", payload={\"n\": i})\ns2.compact()\ntr2 = sorted(s2._layout.trash_dir.iterdir()) if s2._layout.trash_dir.exists() else []\nprint(\"  TRASH after compact() on YOUNG spine:\", [t.name for t in tr2], \"(survives because mtime==now)\")\nprint(\"\\nDONE\")\nPYEOF\ncd /home/kali/sigil && ~/.sigil/venv/bin/python /tmp/claude-1000/-home-kali-Pictures-PENTEST-main/7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27/scratchpad/probe3.py 2>&1","description":"Prove reaper deletes just-trashed aged plaintext"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":9342,"cache_read_input_tokens":100102,"output_tokens":2010,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":9342},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":2010,"cache_read_input_tokens":100102,"cache_creation_input_tokens":9342,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":9342,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CdBw2UqbrNAATkxVnefU9","attributionAgent":"general-purpose","type":"assistant","uuid":"af9ae762-9f29-4011-8f10-61a4fa3b743a","timestamp":"2026-07-19T19:01:21.524Z","effort":"xhigh","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

Here is my review.\n\n---\n\n## RED-PEN #2 verdict: **BLOCK** — one shipped safety mechanism is non-functional in production and its test green-washes it. The gzip/index/tamper core itself is sound.\n\nI built real migrated+rotated+compacted mixed-codec spines (temp `SIGIL_HOME`, `seg_max_records` injected small, then `compact()`) and exercised every helper the brief names. The gz correctness, index, seam, tamper-model and every named consumer **hold** (evidence below). One real defect blocks.\n\n---\n\n### BLOCK-1 — `_reap_trash` ages trash by the segment's original mtime, so the reader-grace window is ZERO in production; the test only passes because its files are milliseconds old (green-wash)\n\n`sigil/spine/store.py:641-653` (`_reap_trash`), driven by `sigil/spine/store.py:637` (`_trash_orphan_plaintext`) and `sigil/spine/store.py:576-577` (`compact()`).\n\n- `_trash_orphan_plaintext` supersedes the plaintext via `os.replace(plain, trash_dir/…)` (line 637). `os.replace` is a **rename** — same inode, so it **preserves the file's mtime** (I confirmed: a file mtime-stamped 2h ago keeps that mtime after rename).\n- `_reap_trash` (line 647-650) computes `cutoff = time.time() - SPINE_TRASH_GRACE_SEC` and deletes any trash file whose **`st_mtime < cutoff`**. That measures *age since the segment was last written (sealed)*, **not** *age since it was trashed*.\n- `compact()` calls `_trash_orphan_plaintext()` then `_reap_trash()` **in the same call** (lines 576-577).\n\n**Concrete input / consequence:** In production, segments are sealed as the spine grows over hours/days; `compact()` is a periodic maintenance op run long after. Any segment sealed more than `SPINE_TRASH_GRACE_SEC` (1h default) before the compaction — i.e. essentially all of them — is moved to trash carrying that old mtime and then **reaped in the same `compact()` call**. I proved it: with sealed segments back-dated 3h (grace 1h), `compact()` leaves `TRASH after compact(): []` — grace window zero. On a young spine (the test's scenario) the identical code leaves the 4 plaintext files intact, purely because their mtime is \"now\".\n\nThis directly contradicts the commit message and the config comment (`config.py:75-77`: \"must exceed the longest lock-free read, so a reader mid-scan is never ENOENT'd\") — readers **are** ENOENT'd immediately; the whole \"MOVE to trash instead of unlink + honor a grace\" design (D2) collapses to \"unlink immediately,\" and the advertised forensic/grace lingering does not exist.\n\n**Green-washed test:** `tests/test_spine_rotation.py:553-567` (`test_reap_trash_respects_grace`). It builds the spine and `compact()`s in one shot, so the trashed files have mtime == now, then asserts `s._reap_trash()` under the default 1h grace leaves them (line 564-565: \"trash within the read-grace is NOT reaped\"). It passes **only** because the files are young; it never ages a segment, so it asserts a property (`grace protects an aged, realistically-superseded segment`) that is false. All 5 new tests pass (`pytest -k \"reap or grace or compact\"` → `.....`), which is exactly why this slipped.\n\n**Blast radius (honest):** I could **not** produce current data loss. On POSIX an open fd survives the reap (same inode), and every reader that resolves-then-opens a vanished plaintext recovers: `iter_records` retries on `FileNotFoundError` (store.py:768-773), `get()` falls through its `except (OSError,…)` to a scan (store.py:794-795), and `tail()/_read_last_entry` resolve paths fresh from the manifest (which is flipped to `.gz` *before* the plaintext is ever moved, so `_segments_in_order` never raises `SpineError` mid-compaction). So the retry net — not the grace — is what actually protects readers, and it holds for today's consumers. But the merge asserts a safety margin (via commit text, a config invariant, and a dedicated test) that provably does not exist, and the moment a future reader opens a resolved segment path outside those guards (or on a non-POSIX FS), this becomes reachable data loss. A shipped, tested safety mechanism that does nothing it claims is block-worthy. Fix: stamp the trash entry's own clock (e.g. `os.utime` on insert, or encode the trash-time in the name and reap on that), and age the test's segments before asserting the grace.\n\n---\n\n### What I verified genuinely HOLDS (so the panel isn't re-deriving it)\n\nAll grounded in probes over real mixed gz+plaintext spines:\n\n- **Decompressed-offset index / `get()` over gz (concern 1):** `get(seq)` for **every** seq across a 7-gz + 1-plaintext spine equals the full-scan record (entry_hash + cert_digest). `gzip.GzipFile.seek(off)+readline()` returns exactly the record `_scan_segment` indexed at decompressed offset `off`. No off-by-one.\n- **`iter_records(since_seq)` seek+iterate mid-gz:** tested every `since` in −1..29; the `f.seek(start_off)` then `for raw in f` over a `GzipFile` yields exactly `seq > since` with no gap/dup.\n- **Reconcile with a gz segment (concern 2):** a gz never hits the active branch (active is always plaintext, is `segs[-1]`). The `elif scanned < size` sealed branch compares decompressed-`scanned` vs compressed-`size`; because real records always compress (even 1-record segments: 426 dec vs 292-326 comp), `scanned < size` is False → **indexed once, 0 re-scans across 6 appends** — confirmed. Re-scanning (if it ever triggered) overwrites offsets identically, so no corruption. (See NOTE-1.)\n- **verify()/tamper-model over gz (concern 3):** `iter_records` is byte-identical across `compact()` (`equal = True`); `verify()` green before and after; `entry_count`/signed head unchanged by compaction and `verify_checkpoint()` re-verifies clean. A tampered-then-re-gzipped record is caught (`binding break at seq 0`). The manifest `bytes`/`sha256` are **not** tamper inputs (manifest is non-load-bearing; `sha256` is never set — concern 7 OK, see NOTE-3).\n- **tail / `_last_line_of` over gz (concern 4):** `tail(k)` for k∈{1,3,7,15,30,40} matches the reference last-k across the gz/plaintext seam. Empty active whose seam boundary is a **gz** sealed segment resolves `next_seq` correctly (8), appends at seq 8, verify green.\n- **Mixed-codec consumers (concern 5):** every named consumer reads via `store.iter_records()` (grepped: killswitch, envelope, mesh/registry, actor_scope, queries) — none touch segment files directly. A kill-switch engaged in an early record buried under appends into a **gz seg-0** reconstructs `is_engaged() == True`, same as pre-compact.\n- **Codec detection by suffix (concern 6):** `segment_filename(id,\"gzip\")` → `…jsonl.gz` (`.suffix==\".gz\"`), plaintext → `.jsonl`; manifest `file`/`codec` and suffix always agree; validator forbids `..`/absolute.\n\n---\n\n### Secondary notes (not blocking)\n\n- **NOTE-1 (latent perf cliff), store.py:982 / 999-1002:** the \"sealed gz indexed once\" invariant relies on `decompressed_len ≥ compressed_len`, an *accidental* property of the data, not enforced. A pathological incompressible sealed segment (decompressed < compressed) would be fully re-decompressed and re-scanned on **every epoch change (every append)**. Unreachable with real records, but the branch compares two different length spaces.\n- **NOTE-2 (get is not O(1) over gz), store.py:776-789 docstring:** `get()` claims \"O(1) via the index.\" For a gz segment `gzip.GzipFile.seek(off)` has no random access — it decompresses from the stream start to `off`, so a `get()` into the last record of a 16 MiB-compressed (~100 MiB decompressed) segment is a near-full-segment decompress. Correct, but the O(1) claim is an overclaim for compacted segments; likewise `_read_segment_tail`/`_last_line_of`/`_scan_segment` `f.read()` the whole decompressed segment into memory.\n- **NOTE-3 (latent), store.py:609:** `_compress_one_sealed` `model_copy(update={\"codec\",\"file\",\"bytes\"})` does **not** touch `Segment.sha256`.
